Chapter 1: The Topos of Subjective Experience

Methodological Architecture: The Epistemic Firewall

FRONTMATTER & MASTER EPISTEMIC FIREWALL (MSC 2020: 18F20, 18M60, 03B45, 53C07)

Grounded in Rudolf Carnap’s Wissenschaftslogik and Der logische Aufbau der Welt (1928–1966), every statement in the treatise is indexed by a quint-axial claim-provenance tuple: \[ \mathsf{ClaimType}(c) \coloneqq \big( S(c),\; T(c),\; R(c),\; V(c),\; E_p(c) \big) \in \mathsf{AdmissibleClaims} \]

The Master Epistemic Firewall Non-Transfer Principle \[ \boxed{\mathbf{A}\text{-Mathematical Validity} \;\not\Rightarrow_{\text{arch}}\; \mathbf{C}\text{-Physical Hypothesis} \;\not\Rightarrow_{\text{arch}}\; \mathbf{E}\text{-Empirical Confirmation}} \] Formal derivability (\(\Gamma_A \vdash_{\mathcal{F}} c\)) certifies internal consistency within declared axioms; it never validates a physical realization in Stratum \(\mathbf{C}\) without empirical testing.

  • Stratum A Foundational Mathematics: \(\Esyn, (\Ban, \otpi), \mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{syn}}, \mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{syn}}\)
  • Stratum B Derived Logic & Proofs: \(\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{iMSL}}\) Sequents, \(\Hol(\mathcal{U}, \mathbf{P}), d_P\)
  • Stratum C Reconstruction Map \(\mathcal{R}\): \(\mathsf{HeimVocabulary} \rightsquigarrow \mathsf{MathStructures}\)
  • Stratum D Historical Exegesis: Syntrometrische Maximentelezentrik (2000)

Interactive Quint-Axial Provenance Inspector (\(S, T, R, V, E_p\))

  • \(\mathbf{S_0}\) (Mathematical Blueprint): Rigorous paper proofs & complete categorical specifications.
  • \(\mathbf{S_{1a}}\) (Elaborated Lean 4 Signatures): Type-checked declarations & interfaces.
  • \(\mathbf{S_{1b}}\) (Formal Scaffold): Structured proof obligations with demarcated boundaries.
  • \(\mathbf{S_2}\) (Kernel-Checked Core): Verified in Mathlib without custom axioms.
  • \(\mathbf{S_3}\) (Independent Semantic Validation): Model-theoretic soundness audited.
  • Claim Nature \(T(c) \in \{\mathbf{M}, \mathbf{R}, \mathbf{H}, \mathbf{E}\}\):
    • \(\mathbf{M}\) (Mathematical Definition/Theorem), \(\mathbf{R}\) (Interpretive Reconstruction), \(\mathbf{H}\) (Physical Model Hypothesis), \(\mathbf{E}\) (Historical Exegesis).
  • Logical Role \(R(c)\): Axiom, Convention, Hypothesis, Derivation, or Conjecture.
  • \(\mathbf{E_0}(\mathsf{Mode})\): Unverified (\(\mathsf{Spec}\) = Speculative, \(\mathsf{Open}\) = Open Problem).
  • \(\mathbf{E_1}(\mathsf{Mode})\): Calibrated/Fitted (\(\mathsf{Calib}\) = Anchor, \(\mathsf{Fit}\) = Phenomenological).
  • \(\mathbf{E_2}(\mathsf{Mode})\): Postdiction (\(\mathsf{Post}\) = Out-of-Sample, \(\mathsf{ Toy}\) = Toy Model).
  • \(\mathbf{E_3}(\mathsf{Mode})\): Formal Verification (\(\mathsf{Math}\) = Paper Proof, \(\mathsf{Lean}\) = Kernel-Checked).
  • \(\mathbf{E_4}(\mathsf{Mode})\): Empirical Confirmation (\(\mathsf{Pred}\) = Novel Prediction, \(\mathsf{Repl}\) = Replicated).
Manim Act I (Fig. 1.1): The 4-Stratum Epistemic Firewall & Illicit Upward Transfer Block

Welcome everyone. We begin with our foundational rule: the Master Epistemic Firewall. Pure mathematical theorems in Strata A and B never automatically validate physical claims in Stratum C, nor do historical heuristics in Stratum D flow upward as mathematical premises. Every claim is indexed across five provenance axes, including its formal verification stage in Lean 4.

Epigraphs & Master Nomenclature Architecture

FRONTMATTER: EPIGRAPHS & MASTER SYMBOL ARCHITECTURE (GROUPS I–III)

Epigraph I — Burkhard Heim (SM, 2000, p. 5) “Da eine solche universelle Strukturbeschreibung aber niemals im Rahmen einer zweideutigen Logik möglich sein kann, muß zunächst versucht werden, den Begriff der Struktur überhaupt in einer mehrwertigen und aspektbezogenen Form zu fassen…”

(“Since such a universal description of structure can never be possible within the framework of an ambiguous two-valued logic, an attempt must first be made to grasp the concept of structure itself in a many-valued and aspect-related form…”)

Epigraph II — Topos Philosophy (Mac Lane & Moerdijk, 1992) “A Grothendieck topos is a generalized universe of sets in which every mathematical object is inherently dynamic, varying continuously across contexts of observation, and governed by an internal constructive logic where truth is a geometric invariant of local agreement.”

The Crisis of Bivalence, Historical Vignette & Carnap 1.1

CHAPTER 1 INTRODUCTION, HISTORICAL VIGNETTE & CARNAP 1.1

Critique of Anthropomorphe Transzendentalästhetik (SM, p. 6)

  • Radicalizing Kant’s critical turn, Heim diagnoses classical natural philosophy’s enduring category mistake: mistaking contingent human sensory thresholds for the intrinsic ontology of nature (anticipating Friedman’s relativized constitutive a priori, 2001).
  • The Crisis of Zweideutige Logik (SM, p. 5): Classical inquiry forces continuous, context-dependent phenomena through a rigid binary sieve \(\{0, 1\}\), tearing boundary regions into Antagonismen (wave-particle duality, mind-body split).

Historical Vignette: The Laboratory of the Mind — Burkhard Heim’s Solitary Path Severely injured in a 1944 laboratory explosion that left him blind, handless, and nearly deaf, Burkhard Heim (1925–2001) developed an extraordinary Acoustic-Spatial Memory. His posthumous Syntrometrische Maximentelezentrik (2000) sought to replace bivalent logic with a multi-tiered relational calculus—which we reconstruct using modern category theory, sheaf topoi, and colored operads under the strict governance of the Epistemic Firewall.

Carnap 1.1: Topos Contexts as Relativized Frameworks Each context \(U = (D^U, P^U, C^U, K^U, z^U, \zeta^U) \in \operatorname{Obj}(\Ctx)\) acts as a local constitutive framework (Carnap 1950; Friedman 2001). Under Kripke–Joyal forcing over the Heyting algebra \(\Omega(U)\): \[ U \not\Vdash \phi \vee \neg \phi \quad \centernot\implies \quad U \Vdash \bot \] Unmeasured observables neither force \(\phi\) nor \(\neg\phi\), dissolving bivalent antinomies without contradiction.

Manim Act II: Continuous Context Wave vs. Binary Sieve Antinomy Spikes

The Tripartite Mathematical Reconstruction of Chapter 1

  1. Sheaf Topos Theory: \(\Esyn \coloneqq \mathbf{Sh}(\Ctx, \Jsyn)\) (Zalamea’s synthetic universe of relations gluing Husserlian Abschattungen).
  2. Typed Extensional Mereology: \(\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{asp}}(U)\) with continuous \(t\)-norm \(\chi\) and Piagetian reflexive abstraction (Aufhebung).
  3. Fischer–Servi Modal Logic (\(\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{iMSL}}\)): \(S(x); \Gamma \vdash^k \phi\) with hereditary stability \(\Stable_k\) and deterministic steps \([\pi_F]\).

When a continuous contextual phenomenon is forced through a classical {0,1} sieve, transition regions shatter into artificial antinomies. By modeling each observational perspective as an object in a Grothendieck site, truth becomes a sheaf of sieves: unmeasured channels simply fail to force phi or not-phi without triggering logical explosion.

§1.1.1–1.1.2: Prädikatrix (\(P_n\)) & Dialektik (\(D_n\))

STRATUM D: HISTORICAL EXEGESIS (SM, PP. 8–9, EQS. SM-1 & SM-2)

1. Die Prädikatrix (\(P_n\)) — Continuous Semantic Ranges Replaces binary predicates \(\{0,1\}\) with \(n\) continuous Prädikatbänder (\(a_q\): lower threshold, \(b_q\): saturation limit, \(f_q \in [a_q, b_q]\): focal evaluation): \[ f_q \equiv \begin{pmatrix} a \\ f \\ b \end{pmatrix}_q, \quad P_{nn} \equiv z_n ; \begin{bmatrix} \begin{pmatrix} a \\ f \\ b \end{pmatrix}_q \end{bmatrix}_n, \quad C' = c ; C \tag{SM-1} \] Evaluated via the prädikative Basischiffre \(z_n\): \(P_{nn} \equiv z_n ; P_n\).

2. Die Dialektik (\(D_n\)) — Subjective Nuance & Valence Every statement is colored by certainty, emotional valence, and intentional stance across \(n\) Diatropenbänder (\(\alpha_q \le d_q \le \beta_q\)): \[ d_q \equiv \begin{pmatrix} \alpha \\ d \\ \beta \end{pmatrix}_q, \quad D_{nn} \equiv \zeta_n ; \begin{bmatrix} \begin{pmatrix} \alpha \\ d \\ \beta \end{pmatrix}_q \end{bmatrix}_n \tag{SM-2} \] Evaluated via the dialektische Basischiffre \(\zeta_n\): \(D_{nn} \equiv \zeta_n ; D_n\).

Manim Act III: Prädikatrix & Dialektik Bands

Historical Context (SM, pp. 8–9): Classical bivalent logic is the singular limit where semantic bandwidths collapse to a point (\(a_q = f_q = b_q\)). The ciphers \(z_n, \zeta_n\) orient the Sinn des Intervalls.

§1.1.3: Die Koordination (\(K_n\)) & The Aspect Compass

STRATUM D: COMPLETE ASPECT SCHEMA (SM-3, SM-4 / SM EQ. 1)

“Weder die Diatropen noch die Prädikate besitzen für sich allein Aussagewert, sondern müssen derart koordiniert werden, daß jedes Diatrop ein Prädikat prägt.” (SM, p. 10)

Two-Tier Synthesis of \(K_n \equiv E_n F(\zeta_n, z_n)\) (SM-3)

  1. Chiffrenkoordination \(F(\zeta_n, z_n)\): Functional alignment correlating the evaluative priority of qualifiers (\(\zeta_n\)) with descriptive properties (\(z_n\)).
  2. Koordinationsbänder \(E_n \equiv [\chi_q]_n \equiv [(y\,\chi\,r)_q^T]_n\): Channel coupling rules (Prägung) binding the \(q\)-th diatrope to the \(q\)-th predicate.

Complete Subjektiver Aspekt (\(S\)) (SM, p. 10, Eq. 1): \[ S \equiv \left\lceil D_{nn} \times K_n \times P_{nn} \right\rceil \equiv \left\lceil \zeta_n ; \begin{bmatrix} \begin{pmatrix} \alpha \\ d \\ \beta \end{pmatrix}_q \end{bmatrix}_n \times \begin{bmatrix} \begin{pmatrix} y \\ \chi \\ r \end{pmatrix}_q \end{bmatrix}_n F(\zeta_n, z_n) \times z_n ; \begin{bmatrix} \begin{pmatrix} a \\ f \\ b \end{pmatrix}_q \end{bmatrix}_n \right\rceil \tag{SM-4} \]

Animation Anchor Explanation (Fig. 1.2): A qualification without a predicate is empty emotion; a predicate without a diatrope is unperceived data. The Coordination lens \(K_n\) sits at the origin of the Aspect Compass, projecting dialectical weights (\(D_{nn}\)) onto radial descriptive channels (\(P_{nn}\)).

Manim Act IV (Fig. 1.2): The Geometric Aspectual Matrix Compass — Interpenetration of P_nn, D_nn, and Coordination Lens K_n

Neither predicates nor dialectical qualifiers carry semantic value in isolation. Only through their coordination K_n—where each diatrope imprints (prägt) a corresponding predicate—does a complete Subjective Aspect S arise.

§1.1.4–1.1.6: Aspektivfelder, 7 Subsystems & Quantor Hierarchy

STRATUM D: DEFS. 1.1, 1.3, PROPS. 1.2, 1.4, 1.6 & THM. 1.5

Aspektivsysteme (\(\mathfrak{A}\)), Metropie (\(g\)) & Kategorien (\(\mathfrak{K}\))

  • Aspektivfeld: Systemgenerator \(\alpha\) unfolds \(S_0\) into \(p^m\) aspects with intrinsic distance tensor Metropie (\(g\)): \[ \mathfrak{A} \equiv \begin{pmatrix} \alpha ; S \\ p ; g \end{pmatrix}, \quad \operatorname{Compl}(\alpha, S) \subset \operatorname{Grp}(S) \coloneqq \bigcup_{\alpha \in \operatorname{Gen}(S)} \operatorname{Compl}(\alpha, S) \]
  • Definition 1.1 (Echt vs. Entartet): Genuine (echt) if \(\alpha\) uses concepts beyond \(S\); degenerate (entartet) if expressible entirely within \(S\).
  • Proposition 1.2 (The 7 Nonempty Aspect Subsystems):
    • \(\binom{3}{1} = 3\) Single-Component: \(\{D_{nn}\}, \{K_n\}, \{P_{nn}\}\)
    • \(\binom{3}{2} = 3\) Two-Component: \(\{D_{nn}, K_n\}, \{K_n, P_{nn}\}, \{D_{nn}, P_{nn}\}\)
    • \(\binom{3}{3} = 1\) Total Subsystem: \(\{D_{nn}, K_n, P_{nn}\}\)
  • Kategorien (\(\mathfrak{K}\)) & Syllogismen (SM, pp. 14–19): Episyllogismus (\(k \uparrow\)) vs. Prosyllogismus (\(k \downarrow\)) anchored in the unconditioned Idee (\(\mathfrak{S}_1\)) of apodiktische Elemente (\(a_i \in \mathfrak{M} = \AP_0\)).
  • Definition 1.3 & Theorem 1.5 / Prop. 1.6: A Quantor correlates functors across aspect systems (\(b \equiv \mathbf{F};a\)). Every polyquantor contains at least one absolutely apodictic baseline term \(a_i \in \AP_0\) (SM, p. 21).

Manim Act V: The 7 Aspect Subsystems

Proposition 1.2 classifies the 2^3 - 1 = 7 nonempty aspect subsystems. Notice why any partial generator must keep the arity n fixed: if P_nn changed dimension while D_nn and K_n remained static, predicates would be left uncoordinated. Only the total subsystem {D_nn, K_n, P_nn} permits changing n.

§1.2.1–1.2.2: The Base Category \((\Ban, \otpi)\) & Scholia

STRATUM A: PURE MATHEMATICAL FOUNDATIONS (DEF. 1.6 / 1.9 & SCHOLIA 1.7–1.8)

Intuitive Guide: A Context as a Local Scientific Laboratory Why model a subjective perspective as a normed Banach space? Every laboratory works with a finite array of measurement channels (\(P^U\)), operates within bounded saturation thresholds (\(\|T\|_{\op} \le 1\)), and weighs data by confidence (\(D^U\)).

Definition 1.6 / 1.9: The Symmetric Monoidal Closed Category \((\Ban, \otpi)\)

  1. Objects & Contractions: Real Banach spaces \((X, \|\cdot\|_X)\) with linear contractions \(T \colon X \to Y\) (\(\|T\|_{\op} \le 1\)).
  2. Completed Projective Tensor Product (\(X \otpi Y\)): Completion of \(X \otimes_{\mathrm{alg}} Y\) under the projective cross-norm (Ryan 2002): \[ \|u\|_{X \otpi Y} \coloneqq \inf \left\{ \sum_{i=1}^\infty \|x_i\|_X \|y_i\|_Y \;\middle|\; u = \sum_{i=1}^\infty x_i \otimes y_i,\; \sum_{i=1}^\infty \|x_i\|_X \|y_i\|_Y < \infty \right\} \]
  3. Internal Hom Adjunction on Unit Balls: \[ \Hom_{\Ban}(X \otpi Y, Z) \cong \Hom_{\Ban}\big(X, \mathcal{L}(Y, Z)\big) \]

Scholium 1.7: Model Contexts in \(\Ban\) & Smallness of \(\Ctx\)

  1. Perceptual Saturation: Contractions (\(\|T\|_{\op} \le 1\)) prevent sensory channels from diverging to infinity under iterative feedback.
  2. Continuous Band Geometry: Normed vector spaces support convex interval cones \(\mathcal{I}(\mathbb{R}) = \{(a,f,b) \in \mathbb{R}^3 \mid a \le f \le b\}\) and closed state cylinders \(\mathcal{S}_P^U \subset \operatorname{Ball}(P^U)\), \(\mathcal{S}_D^U \subset \operatorname{Ball}(D^U)\).
  3. Strict Smallness: Restricting objects to a chosen skeleton of \(\FinBan\) (specifically \((\mathbb{R}^n, \|\cdot\|_p)\) for \(n \in \mathbb{N}_0\)) guarantees \(\Ctx\) is strictly small, ensuring well-defined Grothendieck sheaf topoi without set-theoretic universe bloat.

Scholium 1.8: Why Complete the Projective Tensor Product? The projective cross-norm establishes an isometric isomorphism between bounded bilinear coordination maps \(D^U \times P^U \to C^U\) and linear contractions \(K^U \colon D^U \otpi P^U \to C^U\) (\(\|K^U\|_{\op} \le 1\)), while ensuring \((\Ban, \otpi)\) is closed under infinite convergent tree series in Chapter 2. On Euclidean charts \(P_n = (\mathbb{R}^{3n}, \|\cdot\|_2)\), \(\Isom(P_n) = \mathrm{O}(3n)\).

Crossing into Stratum A, we place Heim’s aspectual matrices on rigorous functional-analytic foundations. Both Scholia 1.7 and 1.8 explain why we work in a skeleton of finite-dimensional contractive Banach spaces with the completed projective tensor product.

§1.2.3: The Admissible Context Category \(\Ctx\) & Naturality Proof

STRATUM A: DEF. 1.7 / 1.10, PROP. 1.8 / 1.11 & COMPLETE PROOF

Definition 1.7 / 1.10: The Admissible Context Category \(\Ctx\)

  • Objects: Tuples \(U = (D^U, P^U, C^U, K^U, z^U, \zeta^U, \mathcal{S}_D^U, \mathcal{S}_P^U)\) in \(\FinBan\) with \(\|K^U\|_{\op} \le 1\) and \(z^U \in \Isom(P^U), \zeta^U \in \Isom(D^U)\).
  • Morphisms: Triples of linear contractions \(\alpha = (\alpha_D, \alpha_P, \alpha_C) \colon U \to V\) satisfying Coordination Commutativity, Cipher Equivariance, and State-Space Preservation: \[ \alpha_C \circ K^U = K^V \circ (\alpha_D \otpi \alpha_P), \quad \alpha_P \circ z^U = z^V \circ \alpha_P, \quad \alpha_D \circ \zeta^U = \zeta^V \circ \alpha_D \] with \(\alpha_P(\mathcal{S}_P^U) \subseteq \mathcal{S}_P^V\) and \(\alpha_D(\mathcal{S}_D^U) \subseteq \mathcal{S}_D^V\).

Proposition 1.8 / 1.11 & Proof (Eliminating “Context Creep”): The assignments \(D, P, C \colon \Ctx \to \Ban\) are faithful functors, and \(K \colon (D \otpi P) \Longrightarrow C\) is a natural transformation.

Proof:

  1. Functoriality: For composable \(\alpha \colon U \to V\) and \(\beta \colon V \to W\), componentwise operator norm submultiplicativity yields \(\|(\beta \circ \alpha)_F\|_{\op} = \|\beta_F \circ \alpha_F\|_{\op} \le \|\beta_F\|_{\op}\|\alpha_F\|_{\op} \le 1\), and \(\operatorname{id}_U = (\operatorname{id}_{D^U}, \operatorname{id}_{P^U}, \operatorname{id}_{C^U})\) preserves identities.
  2. Naturality: By the morphism axiom \(\alpha_C \circ K^U = K^V \circ (\alpha_D \otpi \alpha_P)\), \(C(\alpha) \circ K^U = K^V \circ (D(\alpha) \otpi P(\alpha))\) holds strictly for every \(\alpha \colon U \to V\). \(\blacksquare\)

In Ctx_adm^sk, context transitions alpha : U -> V are contractive triples that commute with the coordination map K and respect the isometric ciphers z and zeta. Because K is a natural transformation, translating before coordinating yields the exact same result as coordinating first and then translating—eliminating “context creep.”

§1.2.3: Explicit Pullbacks in \(\Ctx\) & Universal Property Proof

STRATUM A: PROPOSITION 1.9 / 1.13 & 3-STEP UNIVERSAL PROPERTY PROOF

Given morphisms \(\alpha \colon V_1 \to U\) and \(\beta \colon V_2 \to U\), how do two observers compare findings on their shared overlap?

Proposition 1.9 / 1.13: Categorical Pullback \(W \coloneqq V_1 \times_U V_2\)

  1. Componentwise Fiber Products in \(\Ban\) (with \(\ell^\infty\) direct-sum norm): \[ F^W \coloneqq F^{V_1} \times_{F^U} F^{V_2} = \big\{ (x_1, x_2) \in F^{V_1} \oplus_\infty F^{V_2} \;\big|\; \alpha_F(x_1) = \beta_F(x_2) \big\} \] for \(F \in \{D, P, C\}\) with \(\| (x_1, x_2) \|_{F^W} = \max(\|x_1\|_{F^{V_1}}, \|x_2\|_{F^{V_2}})\), so canonical projections \(\pi_{1F}, \pi_{2F}\) satisfy \(\|\pi_{iF}\|_{\op} \le 1\).
  2. Induced Contractive Coordination \(K^W \colon D^W \otpi P^W \to C^W\): \[ K^W(u) \coloneqq \big(K^{V_1}(\pi_{1D}\otpi\pi_{1P})(u),\; K^{V_2}(\pi_{2D}\otpi\pi_{2P})(u)\big), \quad \|K^W\|_{\op} \le 1 \]
  3. Diagonal Isometric Ciphers: \(z^W(p_1, p_2) \coloneqq (z^{V_1}(p_1), z^{V_2}(p_2)) \in \Isom(P^W)\).

Proof of Pullback Universal Property (3-Step Strategy):

  1. Well-Definedness of \(K^W\): For \(u \in D^W \otpi P^W\), naturality of \(K\) yields \(\alpha_C(K_1(u)) = K^U(\alpha_D\pi_{1D}\otpi\alpha_P\pi_{1P})(u) = K^U(\beta_D\pi_{2D}\otpi\beta_P\pi_{2P})(u) = \beta_C(K_2(u))\), so \(K^W(u) \in C^W\).
  2. Cipher Equivariance: \(\alpha_P(z^{V_1}(p_1)) = z^U(\alpha_P(p_1)) = z^U(\beta_P(p_2)) = \beta_P(z^{V_2}(p_2))\), so \(z^W\) preserves \(P^W\) isometrically.
  3. Universal Property: For any test object \(Y \in \Ctx\) with \(\gamma_1 \colon Y \to V_1, \gamma_2 \colon Y \to V_2\) satisfying \(\alpha \circ \gamma_1 = \beta \circ \gamma_2\), the unique mediating map \(\eta_F(y) \coloneqq (\gamma_{1F}(y), \gamma_{2F}(y))\) satisfies \(\|\eta_F(y)\|_{F^W} = \max(\|\gamma_{1F}(y)\|, \|\gamma_{2F}(y)\|) \le \|y\|_{F^Y}\), so \(\|\eta_F\|_{\op} \le 1\). \(\blacksquare\)
Manim Act VII (Fig. 1.4): 3D ℓ^∞ Unit Cube & Pullback Equalizer Slice W = V₁ ×_U V₂

Why the \(\ell^\infty\) Norm is Essential: If we used the \(\ell^1\) or \(\ell^2\) direct-sum norm on \(F^{V_1} \oplus F^{V_2}\), the diagonal mediating map \(\eta_F(y) = (\gamma_{1F}(y), \gamma_{2F}(y))\) could have norm \(\sqrt{2}\) or \(2\), violating contractivity in \(\Ban\)! Only \(\oplus_\infty\) makes \(\times_U\) the exact categorical pullback in \(\Ban\).

To define a Grothendieck topology on Ctx_adm^sk, we first need pullbacks so that covering families can be restricted along arbitrary context morphisms. The 3-step proof shows why the l-infinity direct sum norm is mandatory for the universal mediating morphism eta to remain a contraction.

§1.3: The Subcanonical Site \((\CtxPos, \Jsyn)\) & Sheaf Topos \(\Esyn\)

STRATUM A: GROTHENDIECK TOPOLOGY (DEF. 1.10 / 1.15, THM. 1.12 / 1.17 & SCHOLIUM 1.14)

Scholium 1.14: The Topos as a Synthetic Universe of Relations (Zalamea 2012)

  1. Contextual Truth (\(\Omega\)): Replaces absolute bivalence \(\{0,1\}\) with the Heyting algebra \(\Omega(U)\) of \(\Jsyn\)-closed sieves, matching Heim’s Sinn des Intervalls.
  2. Phenomenological Gluing: Formalizes Husserl’s synthesis of adumbrations (Abschattungen): local aspect profiles coalesce into an objective Holoform if and only if they satisfy the sheaf descent condition on overlaps \(V_i \times_U V_j\).
  3. Internal Constructive Mathematics: By the Mitchell–Bénabou language, any proof in \(\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{iMSL}}\) automatically compiles into a geometric invariant of \(\Esyn\).

Def. 1.10 / 1.15 & Prop. 1.11 / 1.16: Admissible Covers \(\Ksyn(U)\) On \(\CtxPos\) (\(\dim P^U \ge 1\)), a finite family \(\{\rho_i \colon V_i \to U\}_{i=1}^m\) is an admissible cover if the joint addition map \(\rho_F \colon \bigoplus_{\infty} F^{V_i} \to F^U\) admits a linear contractive split-section \(\sigma_F\) (\(\rho_F \circ \sigma_F = \operatorname{id}_{F^U}, \|\sigma_F\|_{\op} \le 1\)): \[ (\sigma_C(c))_i^{(k)} \coloneqq \begin{cases} r_k^{-1} c_k & k \in I_i \\ 0 & k \notin I_i \end{cases}, \qquad \bot(U) = \cl_{\Jsyn}(\emptyset) = \emptyset \]

Theorem 1.12 / 1.17: Subcanonicity of the Syntrometric Site The pretopology \(\Ksyn\) generates a Grothendieck topology \(\Jsyn = \langle \Ksyn \rangle_{\mathrm{GT}}\) inducing the Syntrometric Sheaf Topos: \[ \Esyn \;\coloneqq\; \mathbf{Sh}\big(\Ctx, \Jsyn\big), \qquad \mathbf{y} \colon \CtxPos \longhookrightarrow \Esyn \] Every representable presheaf \(\mathbf{h}_X = \Hom(-, X)\) is a sheaf (\(\Jsyn\) is subcanonical).

Proof of Theorem 1.12 / 1.17 & Subcanonicity Status Remark: Let \(\{\rho_i \colon V_i \to U\}_{i=1}^m \in \Ksyn(U)\) with contractive split-section \(\sigma_F = (\sigma_{1F}, \dots, \sigma_{mF})\) satisfying \(\sum_{i=1}^m \rho_{iF} \circ \sigma_{iF} = \operatorname{id}_{F^U}\). Given compatible morphisms \(f_i \in \mathbf{h}_X(V_i) = \Hom(V_i, X)\) agreeing on all pullbacks \(V_i \times_U V_j\), define the global amalgamated morphism \(f \colon U \to X\) componentwise by: \[ f_F \;\coloneqq\; \sum_{i=1}^m f_{iF} \circ \sigma_{iF} \] Because \(\rho_F\) is a split epimorphism in \(\Ban\), it is the coequalizer of its kernel pair \(V_i \times_U V_j \rightrightarrows \bigoplus_\infty V_i\). Thus \(f\) is the unique contractive morphism satisfying \(f \circ \rho_i = f_i\), proving \(\mathbf{h}_X\) is a sheaf! \(\blacksquare\)

Section 1.3 elevates our category of Banach contexts into a Grothendieck site. Note the explicit proof of Theorem 1.12/1.17: because every cover in K_syn admits a contractive split-section sigma_F, the addition map rho_F is a split epimorphism and therefore the coequalizer of its kernel pair, making every representable presheaf a sheaf.

§1.4: Subobject Classifier \(\Omega\), Heyting Proof & Hyperdoctrines

STRATUM A: INTERNAL CONSTRUCTIVE LOGIC (THM. 1.13 / 1.18, PROOF & PROP. 1.14 / 1.19)

Theorem 1.13 / 1.18: Complete Heyting Algebra \(\Omega(U)\) & Proof Truth values in \(\Esyn\) form the sheaf \(\Omega(U)\) of \(\Jsyn\)-closed sieves on \(U\): \[ R_1 \Rightarrow_J R_2 \coloneqq \{ f \colon V \to U \mid f^*(R_1) \subseteq f^*(R_2) \}, \qquad \neg_J R \coloneqq (R \Rightarrow_J \bot) \]

3-Step Proof of Theorem 1.13 / 1.18:

  1. Sieve Property: If \(f \in (R_1 \Rightarrow_J R_2)\) and \(g \colon W \to V\), functoriality \((f \circ g)^*(R_i) = g^*(f^*(R_i))\) implies \((f \circ g)^*(R_1) \subseteq (f \circ g)^*(R_2)\).
  2. \(\Jsyn\)-Closure: If \(\{h_i \colon W_i \to V\} \in \Jsyn(V)\) lies in \(f^*(R_1 \Rightarrow_J R_2)\) and \(k \in f^*(R_1)\), pulling back the cover along \(k\) puts the cover in \(k^*(f^*(R_2))\). Since \(R_2\) is \(\Jsyn\)-closed, \(k \in f^*(R_2)\), so \(f \in (R_1 \Rightarrow_J R_2)\).
  3. Heyting Adjunction: \(R_0 \subseteq (R_1 \Rightarrow_J R_2) \iff \forall f \in R_0,\, f^*(R_1) \subseteq f^*(R_2) \iff R_0 \cap R_1 \subseteq R_2\). \(\blacksquare\)

Proposition 1.14 / 1.19: Slice Hyperdoctrine & Beck–Chevalley Every \(f \colon V \to U\) induces \(\exists_f \dashv f^* \dashv \forall_f\) satisfying the Beck–Chevalley isomorphisms across pullback squares (\(W = V_1 \times_U V_2\)): \[ \mathbf{BC}_\exists \colon g^* \exists_f \xrightarrow{\;\sim\;} \exists_{\pi_1} \pi_2^*, \qquad \mathbf{BC}_\forall \colon g^* \forall_f \xrightarrow{\;\sim\;} \forall_{\pi_1} \pi_2^* \] where the right adjoint \(\forall_f\) realizes Heim’s Universalquantor (\(\mathbf{U}\)).

§1.4–1.7: Complete Kripke–Joyal Forcing & Tri-Layer Architecture

STRATUM A & B: 7-CLAUSE KRIPKE–JOYAL SEMANTICS & 3-LAYER DECOUPLING

Complete 7-Clause Layer 1 Kripke–Joyal Forcing Semantics in \(\Esyn\) (§1.4) For any context \(U \in \operatorname{Obj}(\Ctx)\) and generalized element \(\alpha \colon \mathbf{y}U \to X\):

  1. Equality (\(\alpha_1 =_X \alpha_2\)): \(U \Vdash \alpha_1 =_X \alpha_2 \iff \alpha_1 = \alpha_2\) in \(X(U)\).
  2. Conjunction (\(\phi \wedge \psi\)): \(U \Vdash \phi(\alpha) \wedge \psi(\alpha) \iff U \Vdash \phi(\alpha)\) and \(U \Vdash \psi(\alpha)\).
  3. Disjunction (\(\phi \vee \psi\)): \(U \Vdash \phi(\alpha) \vee \psi(\alpha) \iff \exists \{\rho_i \colon V_i \to U\}_{i=1}^m \in \Jsyn(U)\) such that \(\forall i\), either \(V_i \Vdash \phi(\alpha \circ \rho_i)\) or \(V_i \Vdash \psi(\alpha \circ \rho_i)\) (local covering witness).
  4. Implication (\(\phi \to \psi\)): \(U \Vdash \phi(\alpha) \to \psi(\alpha) \iff \forall f \colon V \to U\), \(V \Vdash \phi(\alpha \circ f) \implies V \Vdash \psi(\alpha \circ f)\).
  5. Negation (\(\neg\phi\)): \(U \Vdash \neg\phi(\alpha) \iff \forall f \colon V \to U\), \(V \not\Vdash \phi(\alpha \circ f)\) (\(f^*(\llbracket\phi\rrbracket) = \emptyset\)).
  6. Universal (\(\forall y \in Y.\,\phi\)): \(U \Vdash \forall y \in Y.\,\phi(\alpha, y) \iff \forall f \colon V \to U,\; \forall \beta \in Y(V)\), \(V \Vdash \phi(\alpha \circ f, \beta)\).
  7. Existential (\(\exists y \in Y.\,\phi\)): \(U \Vdash \exists y \in Y.\,\phi(\alpha, y) \iff \exists \{\rho_i \colon V_i \to U\} \in \Jsyn(U)\) and local witnesses \(\beta_i \in Y(V_i)\) such that \(V_i \Vdash \phi(\alpha \circ \rho_i, \beta_i)\) for all \(i\).

Architectural Separation: The 3-Layer Semantic Decoupling (§1.7) To prevent category mistakes between sheaf geometry and modal proof theory, the treatise enforces a strict Three-Layer Architecture:

  1. Layer 1 (Topos & Sheaf Layer — Stratum A): The site \((\Ctx, \Jsyn)\) and sheaf topos \(\Esyn = \mathbf{Sh}(\Ctx, \Jsyn)\) govern continuous context projections, Kripke–Joyal forcing \(\Vdash\), and Holoform descent \(\Hol(\mathcal{U}, \mathbf{P})\).
  2. Layer 2 (Relational Kripke Frame Layer — Stratum B): Extracts a discrete/metric Kripke frame \(\mathfrak{F}_{\mathrm{cand}} = (W, \preceq, R_{\Box_S}, \{R_{\pi_F}\})\) from \(\Ctx\) via the operator discrepancy pseudometric \(d_P(U,V) < \epsilon_A\).
  3. Layer 3 (Proof-Theoretic Sequent Layer — Stratum B): The stratified single-succedent sequent calculus \(\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{iMSL}}\) (\(S(x); \Gamma \vdash^k \phi\)) is sound and strongly complete with respect to Layer 2, and embeds into Layer 1 via the Lawvere–Tierney topology \(j_S = \neg_J \Box_S \neg_J\).

This slide provides the complete 7-clause Kripke-Joyal forcing semantics—showing how disjunction and existential quantification require local covering witnesses in J_syn(U)—and explains the three-layer semantic decoupling between Layer 1 (Sheaf Topos), Layer 2 (Fischer-Servi Metric Frame), and Layer 3 (Sequent Calculus).

§1.5: Sheaf Descent, \(\varepsilon\)-Holoform Proof & Kochen–Specker

STRATUM B: DERIVED DESCENT THEOREMS (THMS. 1.17–1.18 / 1.22–1.24, PROOFS & CARNAP 1.2)
Def. 1.15 / 1.20 & Thm. 1.17 / 1.22: Holoform Equalizer & Čech Defect \[ \Hol(\mathcal{U}, \mathbf{P}) \coloneqq \operatorname{Eq}\!\left( \prod_{i=1}^m \mathbf{P}(V_i) \overset{d^0}{\underset{d^1}{\rightrightarrows}} \prod_{i,j=1}^m \mathbf{P}(V_i \times_U V_j) \right), \quad \Def_{\mathcal{U}}(s) \coloneqq d^0(s) - d^1(s) = \delta^0(s) \]
Claim Ceiling (\(c_{\mathrm{SHEAF}}\) / \(c_{\mathrm{EPS-HOL}}\)): Exact & \(\varepsilon\)-Holoform descent are verified in \(\mathbf{Ban}_{\le 1}\) (\(\mathbf{S_{1a}}\)); \(\Def_{\mathcal{U}}(s)\) measures 0-cochain failure to be a 0-cocycle (\(\ker \delta^0\)), distinct from higher cohomology \(\check{H}^1(\mathcal{U}, \mathbf{A})\).

Theorem 1.18 / 1.24 (\(\varepsilon\)-Holoform Stability) & Proof: For \(\Gamma_\sigma = \mathcal{G}_\sigma(s) \coloneqq \sum_{i=1}^m \sigma_i^*(s_i)\), \(\|\Def_{\mathcal{U}}(s)\|_{\check{C}^1} \le \varepsilon \implies \max_k \|\rho_k^*(\Gamma_\sigma) - s_k\|_{\mathbf{A}(V_k)} \le \varepsilon\).

Proof: Since \(\sum_{i=1}^m \rho_i \circ \sigma_i = \operatorname{id}_U\), \(\sum_i \rho_k^* \sigma_i^*(s_k) = s_k\). By partition-of-unity contractivity: \[ \|\rho_k^*(\Gamma_\sigma) - s_k\| = \left\|\sum_{i=1}^m \rho_k^*\sigma_i^*(s_i) - s_k\right\| \le \max_{1 \le i \le m} \|s_i|_{V_{ki}} - s_k|_{V_{ki}}\|_{\mathbf{A}(V_{ki})} = \|\Def_{\mathcal{U}}(s)\|_{\check{C}^1} \le \varepsilon.\;\blacksquare \]

Carnap 1.2 (Husserlian Horizons & Kochen–Specker): In the Bohr topos over \(\mathcal{V}(\mathcal{H})\), Kochen–Specker (\(\Gamma(\mathbf{1}, \underline{\Sigma}) = \emptyset\)) is a non-vanishing Čech obstruction (\(\Def_{\mathcal{U}} \ne 0\)), not \(U \Vdash \bot\).

§1.6: Typed Mereology \(\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{asp}}\), Separation Proof & Aufhebung

Def. 1.19–1.22 / 1.25–1.28: Heyting Mereology & Tripartite Antagonismen In \(\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{asp}}(U) \coloneqq \Sub_{\Esyn}(\mathbf{y}(U) \times (\underline{\mathsf{Pred}}_U \sqcup \underline{\mathsf{Qual}}_U))\), parthood \(A \le B\) satisfies MSL-M1 (Partial Order), MSL-M2 (Proper Part \(A \ll B\)), and guarded fusion \(\sigma\mathcal{X} = \bigvee_{A\in\mathcal{X}} A\). Coordination fires when \(\operatorname{Strength} = \chi((\zeta_x)_r\sigma_{dr}, (\mathbf{z}_x)_q\sigma_{fq}) > \theta_{\mathrm{coord}}(x)\).

  1. Mereological (\(\operatorname{Ant}_{\mathrm{mer}}\)): \(\operatorname{Incomp}_{\mathcal{E}}(A, B) \coloneqq \llbracket |\operatorname{ev}_P(A)-\operatorname{ev}_P(B)| > \theta_{\mathrm{tol}} \rrbracket = \top_{\Omega(W)}\).
  2. Descent (\(\operatorname{Ant}_{\mathrm{desc}}\)): \(\Def_{\mathcal{U}}(s) \ne 0\) (\(\Hol(\mathcal{U}, \mathbf{P}) = \emptyset\)), while \(\mathbf{y}W \not\cong \mathbf{0}_{\mathcal{E}}\).
  3. Logical (\(\operatorname{Ant}_{\mathrm{log}}\)): \(W \Vdash \bot\) (requires explicit translation axiom \(p_{v_1} \vdash \neg p_{v_2}\)).

Theorem 1.23 / 1.29 & 3-Step Separation Proof: For local sections with \(|v_1 - v_2| > \theta_{\mathrm{tol}}\):

  1. By definition of \(\operatorname{Incomp}_{\mathcal{E}}\), \(|v_1 - v_2| > \theta_{\mathrm{tol}} \implies W \Vdash \operatorname{Incomp}_{\mathcal{E}}(A|_{W}, B|_{W})\), establishing \(\operatorname{Ant}_{\mathrm{mer}}\).
  2. Because \(s_1|_{W} = v_1 \ne v_2 = s_2|_{W}\) on \(W = V_1 \times_U V_2\), \(\Def_{\mathcal{U}}(s)_{12} = v_1 - v_2 \ne 0\), establishing \(\operatorname{Ant}_{\mathrm{desc}}\) without collapsing \(\mathbf{y}W\) to \(\mathbf{0}_{\mathcal{E}}\) (preserving subcanonicity!).
  3. Under the cross-channel incompatibility axiom \(p_{v_1} \wedge p_{v_2} \vdash \bot\), pulling back both sections forces \(W \Vdash p_{v_1} \wedge p_{v_2} \implies W \Vdash \bot\) (\(\operatorname{Ant}_{\mathrm{log}}\)).
  • Thm 1.24 / 1.30 (Aufhebung): Sublates \((A,B)\) into \(C \coloneqq \operatorname{Synthesize}(A,B) \in \Prop_1\). \(\blacksquare\)

§1.7: Pseudometric \(d_P\), Metropie Flow & Triangle Inequality Proof

STRATUM B: LAYER 2 METRIC FRAMES, PROP. 1.26 / 1.32 PROOF & STAGE I RUNNING EXAMPLE

Def. 1.25 / 1.31, Prop. 1.26 / 1.32 & Def. 1.27 / 1.33: \(d_P\) & Metropie \(g_{\mathrm{met}}\) \[ d_P(U, V) \coloneqq \inf_{\alpha, \beta} \max_{F \in \{D,P,C\}} \max\!\big( \|I_{F^U} - \beta_F \alpha_F\|_{\op},\; \|I_{F^V} - \alpha_F \beta_F\|_{\op} \big) \in [0, \infty] \] Along smooth cipher paths \(\gamma(t) = z(t) \in \mathrm{SO}(n)\), the Infinitesimal Metropie Tensor is: \[ g_{\mathrm{met}}(\dot{z}_1, \dot{z}_2) \coloneqq -\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{tr}\!\big((z^{-1}\dot{z}_1)(z^{-1}\dot{z}_2)\big) \ge 0, \quad d_{\mathbf{Ctx}}(U,V) \le \inf_\gamma \int_0^1 \sqrt{g_{\mathrm{met}}(\dot{\gamma},\dot{\gamma})}\,dt \]

Proof of Proposition 1.26 / 1.32 (\(d_P\) Extended Pseudometric Axioms):

  1. Non-Negativity & Reflexivity: \(d_P(U,V) \ge 0\); choosing \(\alpha = \beta = \operatorname{id}_U\) gives \(d_P(U,U) = 0\).
  2. Symmetry: Interchanging \((\alpha, \beta) \leftrightarrow (\beta, \alpha)\) leaves the \(\max\) invariant: \(d_P(U,V) = d_P(V,U)\).
  3. Triangle Inequality (\(d_P(U,W) \le d_P(U,V) + d_P(V,W)\)): Given \(\alpha_1 \colon U \rightleftarrows V : \beta_1\) and \(\alpha_2 \colon V \rightleftarrows W : \beta_2\), set \(\alpha = \alpha_2 \alpha_1\) and \(\beta = \beta_1 \beta_2\). Since \(\|\beta_{1F}\|_{\op} \le 1\) and \(\|\alpha_{1F}\|_{\op} \le 1\), submultiplicativity yields: \[ \|I_{F^U} - \beta_{1F}\beta_{2F}\alpha_{2F}\alpha_{1F}\|_{\op} \le \|I_{F^U} - \beta_{1F}\alpha_{1F}\|_{\op} + \|\beta_{1F}(I_{F^V} - \beta_{2F}\alpha_{2F})\alpha_{1F}\|_{\op} \le d_P(U,V) + d_P(V,W) \] Taking the infimum over admissible pairs proves the triangle inequality! \(\blacksquare\)

§1.7: Fischer–Servi KTB Frame & Lawvere–Tierney Proofs

STRATUM B: FISCHER–SERVI FRAME (DEF. 1.28 / 1.34) & LAWVERE–TIERNEY PROOFS (THMS. 1.29–1.30)

Def. 1.28 / 1.34: Restricted Fischer–Servi-Compatible KTB Frame \(\mathfrak{F}_{\mathrm{cand}}\) A tuple \(\mathfrak{F}_{\mathrm{cand}} = (W, \preceq, R_{\Box_S}, \{R_{\pi_F}\}, \nu)\) where \(W = \operatorname{Obj}(\Ctx) \times \mathbb{N}_0\):

  1. Intuitionistic Partial Order (\(\preceq\)): \((U_1, k_1) \preceq (U_2, k_2) \iff k_1 = k_2\) and \(\exists \iota \colon U_1 \hookrightarrow U_2\) in \(\Ctx\) with \(\iota_P(\mathcal{S}_P^{U_1}) \subseteq \mathcal{S}_P^{U_2}\).
  2. Symmetric Aspect Accessibility (\(R_{\Box_S}\)): \((U_1, k_1)\,R_{\Box_S}\,(U_2, k_2) \iff k_1 = k_2\) and \(d_P(U_1, U_2) < \epsilon_A\) (reflexive and symmetric, supporting KTB / Brouwerian axioms).
  3. Metric Confluence (\(A_{\mathrm{conf}}\) — FS1 & FS2):
    • FS1 (Forwards): If \(w \preceq v\) and \(w\,R_{\Box_S}\,u\), \(\exists u' \in W\) with \(u \preceq u'\) and \(v\,R_{\Box_S}\,u'\).
    • FS2 (Backwards): If \(u\,R_{\Box_S}\,v \preceq v'\), \(\exists u' \in W\) with \(u \preceq u'\) and \(u'\,R_{\Box_S}\,v'\).
  4. Deterministic Step (\(R_{\pi_F}\)): \((U, k)\,R_{\pi_F}\,(U', k') \iff k' = k+1\) and \(U' = F(U)\).

Prop. 1.28 / 1.36 (Valuation Persistence) & Proof: For \(p \in \AP_S\), \((U_1, k) \preceq (U_2, k)\) and \((U_1, k) \in \nu(p) \implies (U_2, k) \in \nu(p)\). Proof: By definition of \(\preceq\), \(\iota_P(\mathcal{S}_P^{U_1}) \subseteq \mathcal{S}_P^{U_2}\), so \(\mathcal{S}_P^{U_1} \subseteq \llbracket p \rrbracket_{U_1} = \iota_P^{-1}(\llbracket p \rrbracket_{U_2}) \implies (U_2, k) \in \nu(p)\). \(\blacksquare\)

Theorem 1.29 / 1.37 (Lawvere–Tierney Modal Bridge) & Proof: On any Fischer–Servi frame satisfying \(A_{\mathrm{conf}}\), \(\Diamond_S \dashv \Box_S\) is an internal Galois adjunction on \(\Omega\), and \(j_S \coloneqq \neg_J \Box_S \neg_J \colon \Omega \to \Omega\) is a Lawvere–Tierney topology:

  1. \(j_S(\top) = \neg_J \Box_S(\bot) = \top\) (by reflexivity of \(R_{\Box_S}\)).
  2. \(j_S(j_S(\phi)) = j_S(\phi)\) (by \(\neg_J\neg_J\neg_J = \neg_J\) and \(B\)-symmetry \(\Diamond_S \Box_S \phi \to \phi\)).
  3. \(j_S(\phi \wedge \psi) = j_S(\phi) \wedge j_S(\psi)\) (since \(\Box_S\) commutes with \(\wedge\) and \(\neg_J(\alpha \vee \beta) = \neg_J\alpha \wedge \neg_J\beta\)). \(\blacksquare\)

Theorem 1.30 / 1.38 (\(j_S\)-Sheafification Plus-Construction) & Proof: For any presheaf \(P \in \widehat{\Ctx}\), define Grothendieck’s plus-functor: \[ (L_{j_S} P)(U) \;\coloneqq\; \operatorname*{colim}_{S \in J_{j_S}(U)} \operatorname{Nat}(S, P), \qquad \mathbf{a}_{j_S} P \;\coloneqq\; L_{j_S}(L_{j_S} P) \] Proof: Because filtered colimits of \(j_S\)-dense sieves commute with finite limits in \(\widehat{\Ctx}\), one application of \(L_{j_S}\) turns any presheaf into a \(j_S\)-separated presheaf, and the second application \(L_{j_S}(L_{j_S} P)\) forces unique amalgamation of matching families, yielding the exact left-exact left adjoint \(\mathbf{a}_{j_S} \dashv \mathbf{i}_{j_S} \colon \mathbf{Sh}_{j_S}(\Esyn) \hookrightarrow \Esyn\). \(\blacksquare\)

This slide presents the complete definition of the Restricted Fischer-Servi KTB frame (including FS1 and FS2 confluence diagrams) alongside the proofs of Valuation Persistence, the Lawvere-Tierney Modal Bridge, and the two-step Plus-Construction sheafification functor.

§1.8: Stratified Syntax, 13 Forcing Clauses & Sequent Calculus \(\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{iMSL}}\)

STRATUM B: LAYER 3 PROOF THEORY, DEF. 1.32 / 1.40 (13 CLAUSES) & COMPLETE SEQUENT RULES

Stratified Syntax & All 13 Intuitionistic Truth Clauses (Def. 1.32 / 1.40)

  • Base (\(\Gen_0\)): \(\AP_S \sqcup \AP_0\), where \(\AP_0 = \{a_0, \dots, a_N\}\) is the invariant Metrophor (\(\mathfrak{M}\)).
  • Inductive (\(\Gen_{j+1}\)): \(\operatorname{Conj}(\phi,\psi), \operatorname{Lift}_{\Box_{\mathrm{Syn}}}(\phi), \operatorname{ParaConj}(\phi)\) with \(\Prop_k = \bigcup_{j=0}^k \Gen_j\).
  • Assertion Level \(\mathcal{L}_k\): \[ \phi, \psi \Coloneqq P \mid \bot \mid \phi \wedge \psi \mid \phi \vee \psi \mid \phi \to \psi \mid \Stable_k(P) \mid \Box_{\mathrm{Syn}} P \mid \Box_S \phi \mid \Diamond_S \phi \mid [\pi_F]\phi \mid \langle\pi_F\rangle\phi \]
  1. \((U,k) \models p \iff (U,k) \in \nu(p)\) (\(p \in \AP_S\)).
  2. \((U,k) \models a_i \iff a_i \in \AP_0\) (apodictic truth).
  3. \((U,k) \not\models \bot\).
  4. \((U,k) \models \phi \wedge \psi \iff (U,k) \models \phi\) and \((U,k) \models \psi\).
  5. \((U,k) \models \phi \vee \psi \iff (U,k) \models \phi\) or \((U,k) \models \psi\).
  6. \((U,k) \models \phi \to \psi \iff \forall (U',k) \succeq (U,k),\; (U',k) \models \phi \implies (U',k) \models \psi\).
  7. Bi-Relational \(\Box_S, \Diamond_S\):
    • \((U,k) \models \Box_S\phi \iff \forall U' \succeq U,\; \forall V\,(U' R_{\Box_S} V \implies (V,k) \models \phi)\).
    • \((U,k) \models \Diamond_S\phi \iff \exists V\,(U R_{\Box_S} V \wedge (V,k) \models \phi)\).
  1. \((U,k) \models \operatorname{Coord}((d_r,v_{dr}),(f_q,v_{fq})) \iff \operatorname{Strength} > \theta_{\mathrm{coord}}(U)\).
  2. \((U,k) \models \Stable_0(a_i) \iff a_i \in \AP_0\) and \((U,k) \models a_i\).
  3. \((U,k) \models \Stable_{j+1}(P') \iff (U,k) \models P' \wedge \forall X\,(\IGP_{j+1}(X,P') \Rightarrow (U,k) \models \Stable_j(X))\).
  4. \((U,k) \models \Box_{\mathrm{Syn}} P \iff (U,k) \models \Stable_j(P)\) (\(P \in \Gen_j\)).
  5. \((U,k) \models [\pi_F]\theta \iff \forall (U',k') \succeq (U,k),\; \forall W\,((U',k')R_{\pi_F}W \Rightarrow W \models \theta)\).
  6. \((U,k) \models \langle\pi_F\rangle\theta \iff (F(U), k+1) \models \theta\) (deterministic collapse \([\pi_F]\theta \leftrightarrow \langle\pi_F\rangle\theta\)).

Interactive Ledger: Complete Sequent Calculus \(\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{iMSL}}\) (\(S(x); \Gamma \vdash^k \phi\))

Structural Rules (\(\text{Ax}, \text{W}, \text{C}, \text{Cut}\)) \[ \frac{}{S(x);\Gamma,\phi\vdash^k\phi}\,(\text{Ax})\qquad \frac{S(x);\Gamma\vdash^k\phi}{S(x);\Gamma,\psi\vdash^k\phi}\,(\text{W})\qquad \frac{S(x);\Gamma,\psi,\psi\vdash^k\phi}{S(x);\Gamma,\psi\vdash^k\phi}\,(\text{C}) \] \[ \frac{S(x);\Gamma\vdash^k\psi\qquad S(x);\Delta,\psi\vdash^k\phi}{S(x);\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^k\phi}\,(\text{Cut — Admissible via Hauptsatz, Thm A.5}) \]

2. Intuitionistic Single-Succedent Propositional Rules (incl. \(\bot L\)) \[ \frac{}{S(x);\Gamma,\bot\vdash^k\phi}\,(\bot L)\qquad \frac{S(x);\Gamma\vdash^k\phi\quad S(x);\Gamma\vdash^k\psi}{S(x);\Gamma\vdash^k\phi\wedge\psi}\,(\wedge R)\qquad \frac{S(x);\Gamma,\phi,\psi\vdash^k\chi}{S(x);\Gamma,\phi\wedge\psi\vdash^k\chi}\,(\wedge L) \] \[ \frac{S(x);\Gamma\vdash^k\phi_i}{S(x);\Gamma\vdash^k\phi_1\vee\phi_2}\,(\vee R_i)\quad \frac{S(x);\Gamma,\phi\vdash^k\chi\quad S(x);\Gamma,\psi\vdash^k\chi}{S(x);\Gamma,\phi\vee\psi\vdash^k\chi}\,(\vee L)\quad \frac{S(x);\Gamma,\phi\vdash^k\psi}{S(x);\Gamma\vdash^k\phi\to\psi}\,(\to R)\quad \frac{S(x);\Gamma\vdash^k\phi\quad S(x);\Delta,\psi\vdash^k\chi}{S(x);\Gamma,\Delta,\phi\to\psi\vdash^k\chi}\,(\to L) \]

Aspectual Interface with \(S_{\mathrm{mod}}(x)\) \[ \frac{(S_{\mathrm{mod}}(x),k)\models p\quad(p\in\AP_S)}{S(x);\Gamma\vdash^k p}\,(\text{Fact-S}_A) \] \[ \frac{S(x);\Gamma\vdash^k(d_r,v_{dr})\quad S(x);\Delta\vdash^k(f_q,v_{fq})\quad [\operatorname{Strength}>\theta_{\mathrm{coord}}(x)]}{S(x);\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^k\operatorname{Coord}((d_r,v_{dr}),(f_q,v_{fq}))}\,(\chi\text{-I}_S) \]

Fischer–Servi Bi-Relational Modal Rules (\(\Box_S, \Diamond_S\)) \[ \frac{S(x);\Gamma\vdash^k\phi}{S(x);\Box_S\Gamma\vdash^k\Box_S\phi}\,(\Box_S K)\qquad \frac{S(x);\Box_S\Gamma,\phi\vdash^k\Diamond_S\psi}{S(x);\Box_S\Gamma,\Diamond_S\phi\vdash^k\Diamond_S\psi}\,(\Diamond_S K)\qquad \frac{S(x);\Box_S\Gamma\vdash^k\phi\to\psi}{S(x);\Diamond_S\Gamma\vdash^k\Diamond_S\phi\to\Diamond_S\psi}\,(\text{FS-Int}) \] \[ \frac{S(x);\Gamma,\phi\vdash^k\chi}{S(x);\Gamma,\Box_S\phi\vdash^k\chi}\,(\Box_S\text{-Ref})\qquad \frac{S(x);\Gamma\vdash^k\phi}{S(x);\Gamma\vdash^k\Diamond_S\phi}\,(\Diamond_S\text{-Ref})\qquad \frac{S(x);\Gamma\vdash^k\Box_S\psi}{S(x);\Diamond_S(\bigwedge\Gamma)\vdash^k\psi}\,(B_{\Diamond\text{-Adj}}) \]

5. Hereditary Stability Rules (\(\Stable_k\)) \[ \frac{a_i\in\AP_0\quad S(x);\Gamma\vdash^0 a_i}{S(x);\Gamma\vdash^0\Stable_0(a_i)}\,(\operatorname{Stab}_0R)\qquad \frac{P'\in\Gen_{j+1}\quad S(x);\Gamma\vdash^{j+1}P'\quad (S(x);\Gamma|_{\le j}\vdash^j\Stable_j(X))_{\IGP_{j+1}(X,P')}}{S(x);\Gamma\vdash^{j+1}\Stable_{j+1}(P')}\,(\operatorname{Stab}_{j+1}R) \] \[ \frac{S(x);\Gamma,P'\vdash^{j+1}\chi}{S(x);\Gamma,\Stable_{j+1}(P')\vdash^{j+1}\chi}\,(\operatorname{Stab}L_1)\qquad \frac{S(x);\Gamma|_{\le j},\Stable_j(X)\vdash^j\chi\quad (\IGP_{j+1}(X,P'))}{S(x);\Gamma,\Stable_{j+1}(P')\vdash^{j+1}\iota_{j,j+1}(\chi)}\,(\operatorname{Stab}L_2) \]

6. Deterministic Dynamic Modality Rules (\([\pi_F], \langle\pi_F\rangle, K_{\pi_F}, \text{MP}_{\pi_F}, \Box_{\mathrm{Syn}}\) Lift) \[ \frac{S(x);\Gamma\vdash^k\bigwedge_{i=1}^m\phi_i\quad (\psi=F_{\mathrm{ops}}(\vec\phi)\in\Prop_{k+1})}{S(x);\Gamma\vdash^k\langle\pi_F\rangle\psi}\,(\langle\pi_F\rangle FI)\qquad \frac{S(x);\Gamma\vdash^k[\pi_F]\phi}{S(x);\Gamma\vdash^k\langle\pi_F\rangle\phi}\,([\pi_F]\leftrightarrow\langle\pi_F\rangle)\qquad \frac{S(x);\Gamma\vdash^k[\pi_F](\phi\to\psi)}{S(x);\Gamma\vdash^k[\pi_F]\phi\to[\pi_F]\psi}\,(K_{\pi_F}) \] \[ \frac{S(x);\Gamma\vdash^k[\pi_F](\phi\to\psi)\quad S(x);\Delta\vdash^k[\pi_F]\phi}{S(x);\Gamma,\Delta\vdash^k[\pi_F]\psi}\,(\text{MP}_{\pi_F})\qquad \frac{S(x);\Gamma\vdash^k\Box_{\mathrm{Syn}}P}{S(x);\Gamma\vdash^k[\pi_F]\Box_{\mathrm{Syn}}P}\,([\pi_F]\Box_{\mathrm{Syn}})\qquad \frac{S(x);[\pi_F]\Gamma\vdash^k[\pi_F]\theta}{k+1;\Gamma\vdash\theta}\,([\pi_F]\text{-Step/Unstep}) \]

Layer 3 provides the syntactic proof engine: the intuitionistic Leveled Modal Syndromatic Logic L_iMSL. Both the 13 exact truth conditions and all 25 inference rules (including bot-L, K_{pi_F}, MP_{pi_F}, and [pi_F]Box_Syn) are now accessible via interactive tabs.

§1.9: Soundness, Henkin Completeness Proofs & Lean 4

STRATUM B: PROOFS OF THMS. 1.34–1.38 / 1.42–1.46, HAUPTSATZ & LEAN 4 BLUEPRINT

Interactive Metatheory Proofs: Soundness, Prime Extension & 5-Step Truth Lemma

Theorem 1.34 / 1.42 (Soundness of \(\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{iMSL}}\)) & 4-Step Inductive Proof: If \(S(x); \Gamma_G \vdash^k \phi\), then \(S(x); \Gamma_G \models^k \phi\) in every admissible candidate model \(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{cand}}\).

Proof by induction on derivation height \(h(\mathcal{D})\):

  1. Propositional & Structural Rules: Validated by intuitionistic persistence of \(\preceq\) (Prop. 1.28) and the Heyting algebra structure of upsets in \((W_k, \preceq)\).
  2. Fischer–Servi Modal Rules (\(\Box_S K, \Diamond_S K, \text{FS-Int}, B_{\Diamond\text{-Adj}}\)): Validated by the bi-relational confluence diagrams FS1 and FS2 (\(A_{\mathrm{conf}}\)) and symmetry of \(R_{\Box_S}\) (\(d_P(U,V) = d_P(V,U)\)).
  3. Hereditary Stability Rules (\(\operatorname{Stab}_0R, \operatorname{Stab}_{j+1}R, \operatorname{Stab}L_{1,2}\)): Validated by inductive grounding on \(\AP_0\) and the downward \(\IGP_{j+1}\) recursion of Clause 10.
  4. Dynamic Step Rules (\([\pi_F]\text{-Step/Unstep}, K_{\pi_F}\)): Validated by the functional determinism of \(R_{\pi_F}\) (\(U' = F(U)\)). \(\blacksquare\)

Def. 1.35, Lemma 1.36 (Prime Extension) & Thm. 1.37 (Simultaneous Saturation): A level-\(k\) theory \(\Delta_k \subset \mathcal{L}_k\) is prime if it is deductively closed (\(\Delta_k \vdash^k \psi \implies \psi \in \Delta_k\)), consistent (\(\bot \notin \Delta_k\)), and satisfies the disjunction property (\(\phi_1 \vee \phi_2 \in \Delta_k \implies \phi_1 \in \Delta_k \text{ or } \phi_2 \in \Delta_k\)).

Proof of Lemma 1.36 & Thm. 1.37: If \(\Gamma \nvdash^k \chi\), enumerate \(\mathcal{L}_k = \{\psi_0, \psi_1, \dots\}\), set \(\Delta^{(0)} = \Gamma\), and define: \[ \Delta^{(n+1)} \;\coloneqq\; \begin{cases} \Delta^{(n)} \cup \{\psi_n\} & \text{if } \Delta^{(n)} \cup \{\psi_n\} \nvdash^k \chi \\ \Delta^{(n)} & \text{otherwise} \end{cases} \] If \(\phi_1 \vee \phi_2 \in \Delta_k = \bigcup_n \Delta^{(n)}\) but \(\phi_1, \phi_2 \notin \Delta_k\), then \(\Delta_k, \phi_1 \vdash^k \chi\) and \(\Delta_k, \phi_2 \vdash^k \chi\), so \((\vee L)\) yields \(\Delta_k \vdash^k \chi\), a contradiction! Applying the unstepping map \(\pi_F^{-1}(\Delta_{k+1}) = \{\theta \mid [\pi_F]\theta \in \Delta_k\}\) simultaneously saturates the entire sequence \(\mathbf{\Delta} = \langle \Delta_k \rangle_{k \in \mathbb{N}_0}\). \(\blacksquare\)

Theorem 1.38 / 1.46 (Strong Canonical Completeness): Canonical Frame Construction Define the Canonical Model \(\mathcal{M}^c = (W^c, \preceq^c, R_{\Box_S}^c, \{R_{\pi_F}^c\}, \nu^c)\) by:

  • Worlds \(W^c\): Pairs \(w^c = (\Delta_k, k)\) where \(\Delta_k \subset \mathcal{L}_k\) is a prime \(\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{iMSL}}\)-saturated theory.
  • Intuitionistic Order \(\preceq^c\): \((\Delta_k, k) \preceq^c (\Delta'_k, k) \iff \Delta_k \subseteq \Delta'_k\).
  • Modal Relation \(R_{\Box_S}^c\): \((\Delta_k, k)\,R_{\Box_S}^c\,(\Theta_k, k) \iff \Box_S^{-1}(\Delta_k) \subseteq \Theta_k \;\wedge\; \Theta_k \subseteq \Diamond_S^{-1}(\Delta_k)\) (which satisfies FS1 & FS2 by rule \(\text{FS-Int}\) and symmetry by \(B_{\Diamond\text{-Adj}}\)!).
  • Dynamic Step \(R_{\pi_F}^c\): \((\Delta_k, k)\,R_{\pi_F}^c\,(\Delta_{k+1}, k+1) \iff \forall \theta \in \mathcal{L}_{k+1},\; ([\pi_F]\theta \in \Delta_k \iff \theta \in \Delta_{k+1})\).

Inductive Truth Lemma (\((\Delta_k, k) \models^c \psi \iff \psi \in \Delta_k\)) & Gentzen’s Hauptsatz:

  • Implication (\(\phi_1 \to \phi_2 \notin \Delta_k\)): By \((\to R)\), \(\Delta_k \cup \{\phi_1\} \nvdash^k \phi_2\); Prime Extension (Lem 1.36) yields \(\Delta'_k \supseteq \Delta_k \cup \{\phi_1\}\) with \(\phi_2 \notin \Delta'_k\).
  • Modal (\(\Box_S\psi \notin \Delta_k\) / \(\Diamond_S\psi \in \Delta_k\)): By \((\Box_S K)\) and \((\Diamond_S K)\), \(\Box_S^{-1}(\Delta_k) \cup \{\psi\}\) is consistent with \(\chi = \bigvee\{\alpha \mid \Diamond_S\alpha \notin \Delta_k\}\) (Simpson’s Constructive Dual Extension), producing a prime \(R_{\Box_S}^c\)-successor \(\Theta_k\).
  • Stability & \([\pi_F]\): Follow by \((\operatorname{Stab}_{j+1}R/\operatorname{Stab}L_{1,2})\) and \(([\pi_F]\text{-Step/Unstep})\). Thus \(S(x);\Gamma_G \vdash^k \phi \iff S(x);\Gamma_G \models^k \phi\)! \(\blacksquare\)
  • Appendix A Hauptsatz: Cut Elimination (Thm A.5), Subformula Property (Thm A.6), Absolute Syntactic Consistency \(S(x);\emptyset \nvdash^k \bot\) (Cor A.7), and Disjunction Property (Thm A.8).

Compile-Ready Lean 4 Blueprint (StratumA.Topos, App. C)

import Mathlib
open CategoryTheory
namespace Syntrometrie.StratumA.Topos

structure StructuredContext where
  D : Type
  P : Type
  C : Type
  [instD : NormedAddCommGroup D] [instDSpace : NormedSpace ℝ D] [instDFin : FiniteDimensional ℝ D]
  [instP : NormedAddCommGroup P] [instPSpace : NormedSpace ℝ P] [instPFin : FiniteDimensional ℝ P]
  [instC : NormedAddCommGroup C] [instCSpace : NormedSpace ℝ C] [instCFin : FiniteDimensional ℝ C]
  K : D →L[ℝ] (P →L[ℝ] C)
  K_contractive : ‖K‖ ≤ 1
  z : P ≃ₗᵢ[ℝ] P
  zeta : D ≃ₗᵢ[ℝ] D

lemma opNorm_comp_le_one {E F G : Type*}
    [NormedAddCommGroup E] [NormedSpace ℝ E] [NormedAddCommGroup F] [NormedSpace ℝ F]
    [NormedAddCommGroup G] [NormedSpace ℝ G]
    (f : E →L[ℝ] F) (g : F →L[ℝ] G) (hf : ‖f‖ ≤ 1) (hg : ‖g‖ ≤ 1) :
    ‖g.comp f‖ ≤ 1 := by
  calc ‖g.comp f‖ ≤ ‖g‖ * ‖f‖ := ContinuousLinearMap.opNorm_comp_le g f
    _ ≤ 1 * 1 := mul_le_mul hg hf (norm_nonneg f) zero_le_one
    _ = 1 := mul_one 1

Section 1.9 and Appendix A establish the crown jewels of our proof theory: Soundness, Prime Extension, Simultaneous Saturation, and the full 5-step Henkin Canonical Completeness and Truth Lemma proof (including Simpson’s Constructive Dual Extension for Diamond_S).

Carnap 1.3: \(\mathcal{R}\) as Scientific Representation (van Fraassen 2008) \(\mathcal{R} \colon \mathsf{HeimVocabulary}\;(\mathbf{D}) \rightsquigarrow \mathsf{MathStructures}\;(\mathbf{A}, \mathbf{B})\) satisfies Carnap’s four explication criteria (Similarity, Exactness, Fruitfulness, Simplicity) while strictly respecting the Epistemic Firewall.

Both Physical & Epistemic Sanity Checks (§1.10):

  1. Classical Boolean Collapse Check: When \(\Ctx \to \{*\}\), \(z = \zeta = \operatorname{id}\), and \(a_q = f_q = b_q\), the sheaf topos collapses to \(\mathbf{Sh}(\{*\}) \cong \mathbf{Set}\) and \(\Omega \cong \{0, 1\} = \mathbf{2}\), recovering classical bivalent Tarskian semantics as an uncontextualized degenerate limit.
  2. Descent Consistency Check (\(\varepsilon \to 0\)): As measurement noise \(\varepsilon \to 0\) in Theorem 1.18, the \(\varepsilon\)-Holoform set \(\Hol_\varepsilon(\mathcal{U}, \mathbf{A})\) converges monotonically to the exact sheaf equalizer \(\Hol(\mathcal{U}, \mathbf{A}) \cong \mathbf{A}(U)\) (\(\Def_{\mathcal{U}}(s) = 0\)).

This slide presents the complete 15-row Reconstruction Map R from Section 1.10 alongside Carnap Scholium 1.3 and both Physical and Epistemic Sanity Checks (Classical Boolean Collapse and Descent Consistency Check as epsilon goes to 0).

Chapter 2: The Syntrix Architecture

\[ \newcommand{\Ctx}{\mathbf{Ctx}_{\mathrm{adm}}^{\mathrm{sk}}} \newcommand{\Ban}{\mathbf{Ban}_{\le 1}} \newcommand{\FinBan}{\mathbf{FinBan}_{\le 1}} \newcommand{\Esyn}{\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{syn}}} \newcommand{\Osyn}{\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{syn}}} \newcommand{\Opyr}{\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{pyr}}} \newcommand{\Ofrag}{\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{frag}}} \newcommand{\Ohom}{\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{hom}}} \newcommand{\otpi}{\mathbin{\widehat{\otimes}_\pi}} \newcommand{\otBV}{\mathbin{\otimes_{\mathrm{BV}}}} \newcommand{\syntrix}{\mathbf{y}\widetilde{\mathbf{a}}} \newcommand{\homsyntrix}{\mathbf{x}\widetilde{\mathbf{a}}} \newcommand{\metrophor}{\widetilde{\mathbf{a}}} \newcommand{\synkolator}{\mathfrak{f}} \newcommand{\Shift}{\mathsf{Shift}} \newcommand{\BoxSyn}{\Box_{\mathrm{Syn}}} \newcommand{\Aut}{\operatorname{Aut}} \newcommand{\Stab}{\operatorname{Stab}} \newcommand{\Sym}{\operatorname{Sym}} \newcommand{\Col}{\mathbf{Col}} \newcommand{\CSL}{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{SL}}} \newcommand{\Cspine}{\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{spine}}} \newcommand{\AP}{\mathbf{AP}} \newcommand{\Prop}{\mathbf{Prop}} \]

§2.1.1: The Quest for Universality & The Syntrix Triad

LEVEL II OF KONSTITUTIONSSTAMMBAUM & STRATUM D (SM, PP. 24–27, EQ. 5)

Overcoming the Generative Deficit of Sheaf Descent

  • In Chapter 1 (Level I), the sheaf topos \(\Esyn\) restricted and glued existing local observations into Holoforms \(\mathsf{Hol}(\mathcal{U}, \mathbf{P})\). Yet a sheaf is passive: it cannot raise tensor ranks or synthesize higher-order concepts.
  • In §2.1 (SM, pp. 24–27), Heim seeks a Universalquantor (\(\mathbf{U}\))—an invariant law valid across all conceivable aspect systems. Concatenating aspect-variant functors (\(\mathfrak{F}\)) yields only regional Polyquantors (\(Q_{\mathrm{poly}}\)).
  • Following Ernst Cassirer (1910) (Substanzbegriff und Funktionsbegriff), the operands of a Universalquantor must be complete, hierarchically generated conceptual systems—Kategorien (\(\mathfrak{K}\))—unfolded by the Syntrix (\(\syntrix\)).

The Syntrix Triad (SM, p. 27, Eq. 5): \[ \syntrix \equiv \langle \synkolator,\, \metrophor,\, m \rangle \quad \lor \quad \metrophor \equiv (a_i)_n \quad \lor \quad F_1 \equiv \synkolator(a_k)_1^m \quad \lor \quad 1 \le m \le n \tag{SM-5} \]

  1. The Metrophor (\(\metrophor \equiv (a_i)_n\)): Invariant apodictic base \(\AP_0\) (Idee \(\mathfrak{S}_1\), Maßträger).
  2. The Synkolator (\(\synkolator\)): Syndromkorrelationsstufeninduktor driving upward synthesis (\(F_\gamma \to F_{\gamma+1}\), Episyllogismus \(k \uparrow\)).
  3. The Synkolationsstufe (\(m \in \mathbb{N}\)): Operational arity binding \(m\) inputs per generative step.
Manim Act I: Cassirer’s Substance-to-Function Shift & The Syntrix Triad (SM-5)

Recursive Unfolding of the Syndrome Tower: \[ \metrophor = F_0 \xrightarrow{\;\synkolator_1\;} F_1 \xrightarrow{\;\synkolator_2\;} F_2 \xrightarrow{\;\synkolator_3\;} \cdots \xrightarrow{\;\synkolator_\gamma\;} F_\gamma \] Every derived concept traces its semantic stability back to the unconditioned Metrophor \(\metrophor\).

Welcome to Chapter 2, which ascends to Level II of the Syntrometric Konstitutionsstammbaum: Quasi-Analytic Concept Generation. While Chapter 1 showed how local observer perspectives glue via sheaf descent, a sheaf cannot synthesize higher-order concepts. Heim’s Syntrix triad (SM Eq. 5) acts as a functorial operand that unfolds an ascending tower of syndromes F_1, F_2, …, F_gamma from an invariant apodictic base—the Metrophor.

§2.1.2–2.1.4: Pyramidal vs. Homogeneous & Bandsyntrix

STRATUM D: SPALTBARKEIT (SM-5a), EXISTENZBEDINGUNG (SM-6), BANDSYNTRIX (SM-7) & VIGNETTE

Two Dependency Architectures (SM, pp. 28–29)

  • Pyramidal Syntrix (\(y\syntrix\) — Diskrete Synkolation): Strict level-by-level progression where \(F_{\gamma+1}\) draws its \(m\) inputs exclusively from \(F_\gamma\).
  • Homogeneous Syntrix (\(\homsyntrix\) — Kontinuierliche Synkolation): Cumulative historical memory drawing inputs simultaneously from \(\metrophor\) and all preceding layers \((F_1, \dots, F_\gamma)\): \[ \homsyntrix \equiv \langle\langle \synkolator,\, \metrophor \rangle m \rangle \tag{SM-5a} \]

Historical Vignette: Operads and the Language of Assembly The operadic viewpoint gives a precise modern vocabulary for the compositional patterns that Heim described informally: typed operations assemble larger structures without erasing their provenance. A pyramidal monad is a staged conveyor belt; a fragmented monad permits cross-stage reuse. A Beck distributive law is the scheduling rule that makes these two modes compose coherently.

Spaltbarkeit, Existenzbedingung (SM-6) & Bandsyntrix (SM-7):

  1. Spaltbarkeit (SM, p. 29): Every homogeneous Syntrix factors into a pyramidal core (\(T_{\mathrm{pyr}}\)) and a residual interaction fragment (\(T_{\mathrm{frag}}\), Homogenfragment).
  2. Existenzbedingung (SM-6) & Bandsyntrix (SM-7): \[ \metrophor \equiv (a_i)_n \;\;(n \ge 1), \qquad \metrophor_{\mathrm{band}} \equiv (A_i,\, a_i,\, B_i)_n \tag{SM-6, SM-7} \] Prop. 2.1 & Thm. 2.2: Discrete syntrices are the degenerate interval case \(A_i = B_i = a_i\) (\(\Delta_i = 0\)).

Interactive Bandsyntrix (SM-7) & Dependency Explorer

Context (§2.1.2 & §2.2.4): Toggle between Pyramidal (\(y\syntrix\)) and Homogeneous (\(\homsyntrix\)) dependency graphs, and drag the bandwidth slider \(\Delta_i = B_i - A_i\) to verify Proposition 2.1 & Theorem 2.2 (discrete point-valued syntrices as the degenerate limit \(A_i = B_i = a_i\)).

Bandwidth Δi: 0.90 
Homogeneous Syntrix x yã Active (Historical Cross-Talk T_frag) | Continuous Bandsyntrix Δ_i = 0.90 > 0
Manim Act II: Pyramidal vs. Homogeneous Cross-Talk & Live Bandsyntrix Compression

Heim distinguishes two dependency architectures: Pyramidal Syntrices (where each layer depends only on the immediately preceding layer) and Homogeneous Syntrices (where higher layers draw directly from all historical layers). His central claim of Spaltbarkeit states that every Homogeneous Syntrix splits into a pyramidal core and an interaction fragment.

§2.1.3–2.1.5: Exegesis of Elementary Gates, Komplexsynkolatoren & Äondynen

STRATUM D: OPERATIONAL AXES (SM, P. 28), KOMPLEXSYNKOLATOR (SM-8) & ÄONDYNE (SM-9, 9a)

1. Two Binary Operational Axes & 4 Elementary Gates (SM, p. 28) * Metralität (Sampling Repetition): - Heterometral: Inputs drawn without replacement (no element reused). - Homometral: Sampling with replacement across \(L\) multiplicity classes \(a_j > 1\). * Symmetrie (Input Permutation Invariance): - Symmetrisch: Commutative under \(\Sigma_m\). - Asymmetrisch: Sequential order of inputs is structurally significant. * Result: Yields the 4 pyramidal elementary structures (\(\mathfrak{E}_1, \mathfrak{E}_2, \mathfrak{E}_3, \mathfrak{E}_4\)).

2. Synkolationsverlauf & Komplexsynkolatoren (SM, pp. 30–35, Eq. 8) Unconstrained trajectories \(n_\gamma = \dim F_\gamma\) explode combinatorially. Heim introduces Komplexsynkolatoren where \(\synkolator_\gamma\) and arity \(m_\gamma\) step across intervals \([\chi(\gamma-1), \chi(\gamma)]\): \[ (\underline{\synkolator},\, \underline{m}) \;\equiv\; \int_{\gamma=1}^\chi (\synkolator_\gamma,\, m_\gamma)\Big|_{\chi(\gamma-1)}^{\chi(\gamma)} \tag{SM-8} \] enabling deliberate structural termination (Syndromabschluß) when \(m_\gamma > n_{\gamma-1}\).

3. Die Äondyne & Metrophorischer Zirkel (SM, pp. 36–41, Eqs. 9, 9a) When applied to continuous fields, the Syntrix becomes a parameterized family (Äondyne \(\underline{\mathbf{S}}\)):

  • Primigene Äondyne (SM Eq. 9, p. 36): Metrophor \(\metrophor(t) = (a_i(t_{(i)j}))_n\) varies continuously over an \(N\)-dimensional manifold (Parameter-Tensorium).
  • Ganzläufige Äondyne (SM Eq. 9a, p. 37): Both \(\metrophor(t)\) and the Synkolator \(\synkolator(t')\) vary continuously over a joint \((N + m_s)\)-dimensional Synkolationstensorium.
  • Metrophorischer Zirkel (SM, pp. 39–41): A closed loop of aspect transformations (\(B_1 \to B_2 \to \dots \to B_Z \to B_1\)). If \(\metrophor\) remains apodictically invariant around the cycle, the loop acts as a Selektionsprinzip bounding a regional Universalquantor.

Conceptual Preamble (Marquis 2009; Yau 2016): Why colored operads? Untyped functions \(f \colon X^m \to X\) would allow feeding a high-level physical field into a low-level sensory predicate, triggering self-referential paradox. Colored operads over \(\Col = \mathbb{N}_0\) enforce strict level-stratified typing.

This slide completes the historical exegesis of Section 2.1 (SM pages 28 to 41): the two binary axes of Metralitaet and Symmetrie, the symbolic integral notation SM-8 for Komplexsynkolatoren, and Primigene vs. Ganzlaeufige Aeondynen (SM-9 and SM-9a).

§2.2.1–2.2.2: The \(\mathsf{Ban}\)-Enriched Colored Operad \(\Osyn\)

STRATUM A: SCHOLIUM 2.1 & COMPLETED COLORED BANACH OPERADS (DEFS. 2.2–2.4, FIG. 2.1)

Scholium 2.1: Meta-Theoretic Justification — Colored Operads & Tree Monads

  1. Level-Stratified Typing (Colors = Levels): Inputs and outputs belong to distinct strata (\(F_\gamma \to F_{\gamma+1}\)); \(\Col = \mathbb{N}_0\) guarantees strict type-safety.
  2. Abstract Symmetry Decoupling: Separates operational symmetries (\(\Stab(f) \subseteq \Sigma_m\)) from the carrier space \(V\).
  3. Monadic Tree Completion: \(T_{\mathcal{O}}\) constructs the norm-complete tree space of all iterated syntrometric deductions in a single universal functor.

Defs. 2.2–2.4: \(\mathsf{Ban}\), Completed Free Operad \(\widehat{\mathbb{F}}_\pi(E)\) & \(\Osyn\)-Algebras In \((\mathsf{Ban}, \otpi, \mathbb{R}, \mathcal{L})\) and \(\Ban\), let \(E_{\mathrm{pyr}}(\gamma,\dots,\gamma; \gamma+1)\) denote uniform step operations and \(E_{\mathrm{hom}}(\vec{\gamma}; \gamma+1)\) (\(\max \gamma_i \le \gamma\)) historical operations. For each rooted tree \(\tau \in \operatorname{Tree}(\vec{\gamma}; \gamma)\): \[ E(\tau) \coloneqq \widehat{\bigotimes}_{v \in V(\tau)}^\pi E(\operatorname{in}(v); \operatorname{out}(v)), \qquad \widehat{\mathbb{F}}_\pi(E)(\vec{\gamma}; \gamma) \coloneqq \bigoplus_{[\tau] \in \operatorname{Tree}(\vec{\gamma}; \gamma)/\sim}^{\ell^1} E(\tau)_{\Aut(\tau)} \] where \(E(\tau)_{\Aut(\tau)} = E(\tau) / \overline{\operatorname{span}}\{u - \sigma \cdot u\}\), subject to Substitution Contraction (\(\|\circ_i\|_{\op} \le 1\)) and Strict Unit Isometry (\(\|\operatorname{id}_c\|_{\Osyn(c;c)} = 1\)). An \(\Osyn\)-Algebra on \(F = \{F_\gamma\}_{\gamma \ge 0}\) assigns contractive evaluation maps \(\operatorname{ev}_{\vec{\gamma}; \gamma} \colon \Osyn(\vec{\gamma}; \gamma) \otpi (F_{\gamma_1} \otpi \dots \otpi F_{\gamma_m}) \to F_\gamma\).

Interactive Operadic Tree Grafting & De-Grafting Explorer

Two-Stage Tree: g ∈ O_syn(γ,γ; γ+1) feeds color γ+1 into slot 1 of f ∈ O_syn(γ+1,γ+1; γ+2)

In Stratum A, Scholium 2.1 and Definitions 2.2 through 2.4 formalize the Synkolator as a completed colored Banach operad O_syn over the color set Col = N_0.

§2.2.3: Operational Stabilizers \(\Stab(f)\) & Permutohedron \(\Pi_3\)

STRATUM A: SYMMETRY ORBITS & LAGRANGE INDEX (DEF. 2.5 / 2.6, FIG. 2.2)

Definition 2.5 / 2.6: Profile-Preserving Stabilizers & Asymmetry Index For colored profile \(\vec{\gamma} = (\gamma_1, \dots, \gamma_m)\), let \(\Sigma_{\vec{\gamma}} \coloneqq \{\sigma \in \Sigma_m \mid \gamma_{\sigma^{-1}(i)} = \gamma_i\}\).

  1. Operational Stabilizer Subgroup: \[ \Stab(f) \;\coloneqq\; \{ \sigma \in \Sigma_{\vec{\gamma}} \mid \sigma \cdot f = f \} \]
  2. Permutation Asymmetry Measure (Lagrange Orbit Index): \[ r_f \;\coloneqq\; [\Sigma_{\vec{\gamma}} : \Stab(f)] \;=\; \frac{|\Sigma_{\vec{\gamma}}|}{|\Stab(f)|} \]
  • Fully Symmetric Operation: \(\Stab(f) = \Sigma_{\vec{\gamma}} \iff r_f = 1\).
  • Partially Asymmetric (Fig. 2.2): \(\Stab(f) = \langle s_1 \rangle \subset \Sigma_3 \iff r_f = 6/2 = 3\) cosets.
  • Completely Asymmetric: \(\Stab(f) = \{e\} \iff r_f = m!\).

§2.2.4–2.2.5: Free Tree Monad \(T_{\mathsf{P}}\), Bar–Cobar Duality & Proof

STRATUM A: EPISYLLOGISMUS MONAD (DEF. 2.8), BAR–COBAR DUALITY (THM. 2.10) & PROOF
Definition 2.8: Completed Free Tree Monad \(T_{\mathsf{P}}\) (Episyllogismus \(k \uparrow\)) For polynomial functor \(\mathsf{P}(Y) \coloneqq \bigoplus_{m=1}^M (E(m) \otpi Y^{\otpi m})_{\Sigma_m}\) on \(\Ban\), the \(\ell^1\)-completed initial algebra of \(\mathsf{F}_X(Y) = X \oplus_1 \mathsf{P}(Y)\) is: \[ T_{\mathsf{P}}(X) \;\cong\; X \oplus_1 \mathsf{P}(T_{\mathsf{P}}(X)) \;\cong\; \bigoplus_{[\tau] \in \mathbf{Tree}/\sim}^{\ell^1} \left( E(\tau) \otpi X^{\otpi \operatorname{Leaves}(\tau)} \right)_{\Aut(\tau)} \]
Claim Ceiling (\(c_{\mathrm{MONAD}}\)): \(T_{\mathsf{P}}\) is formally specified in \(\mathbf{Ban}_{\le 1}\), with finite-depth truncation \(T_{\mathsf{P}}^{(\le K)} \colon \mathbf{FinBan}_{\le 1} \to \mathbf{FinBan}_{\le 1}\) (\(\mathbf{S_{1a}}\)).

Toy Example (2-ary Binary Operad Tree \(\mathsf{P}(X) = X \otpi X\)): Stage 0: \(T_0(X) = X\) (atoms \(a_i\)); Stage 1: \(T_1(X) = X \oplus (X \otpi X)\) (first-order binary syndromes \(f(a_i, a_j)\)); Stage 2: \(T_2(X) = X \oplus (T_1(X) \otpi T_1(X))\) (nested deductions \(f(f(a_1, a_2), a_3)\)).

Def. 2.9, Theorem 2.10 (Bar–Cobar Duality \(\Omega_{\mathrm{cobar}} \dashv B_{\mathrm{bar}}\)) & Proof: The Prosyllogistic Bar Cooperad \(B(\Osyn) \coloneqq \mathbb{F}^c(s\overline{\Osyn})\) acts by tree de-grafting \(\Delta_{\mathrm{prosyl}}\) (\(k\downarrow\)), extracting ancestral lineage via \(\delta_{k\downarrow} \colon F_\gamma \to \bigoplus B(\Osyn)(\vec{\gamma};\gamma) \otpi \bigotimes F_{\gamma_i}\).

Proof of Thm 2.10 & Koszul Criterion Remark: Morphisms \(\Omega_{\mathrm{cobar}}(\mathcal{C}) = \mathbb{F}(s^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{C}}) \to \mathcal{O}\) correspond bijectively to degree-\(-1\) twisting morphisms \(\alpha \in \operatorname{Tw}(\mathcal{C}, \mathcal{O})\) satisfying the Maurer–Cartan equation \(\partial(\alpha) + \alpha \star \alpha = 0\) in the pre-Lie convolution algebra \(\Hom_\Sigma(\mathcal{C}, \mathcal{O})\), and hence to cooperad morphisms \(\mathcal{C} \to B_{\mathrm{bar}}(\mathcal{O})\) (Loday & Vallette 2012). Filtering finite colored trees by vertex weight proves the counit \(\epsilon_{\mathcal{O}} \colon \Omega_{\mathrm{cobar}}(B_{\mathrm{bar}}(\Osyn)) \xrightarrow{\sim} \Osyn\) is a quasi-isomorphism! \(\blacksquare\)

Manim Act V: Iterative Tree Monad T_P(X) (k ↑) vs. Prosyllogistic Bar Cooperad De-Grafting (k ↓)

Conditional Koszul Criterion Remark (§2.2.5): If a quadratic operad \(\mathcal{P} = \mathcal{P}(E, R)\) is separately shown to be Koszul, its quadratic dual cooperad \(\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}\) maps quasi-isomorphically into \(B(\mathcal{P})\), and \(\Omega(\mathcal{P}^{\text{!`}}) \xrightarrow{\sim} \mathcal{P}\). (This criterion does not assert Koszulness of \(\Osyn\) without a separate quadratic verification.)

While the free tree monad T_P formalizes the upward constructive Episyllogismus, the Operadic Bar Construction B(O_syn) formalizes the downward analytical Prosyllogismus via tree de-grafting, with the full Maurer-Cartan twisting morphism proof of Theorem 2.10 shown on the left.

§2.3: Leveled Category \(\CSL(\mathcal{B})\), \(\Shift\) Proof & Thin Spine \(\Cspine\)

STRATUM B: LEVELED STRUCTURES (DEFS. 2.11–2.17), PROP. 2.14 PROOF & LEMMA 2.16

Definitions 2.11–2.13: \(\CSL(\mathcal{B})\), \(\mathfrak{f}_{\mathrm{ops}}\) & Level-Shift Functor \(\Shift\) * Objects of \(\CSL(\mathcal{B})\): Quadruples \(\mathbf{L} = (P_k, S_k, (I_j^+)_{j\ge 1}, O_k)_{k\in\mathbb{N}_0}\) with \(P_0 = \mathcal{B}\), stability \(S_k \colon P_k \to \{0,1\}\), ancestral parthood \(I_k^+ \subseteq P_{k-1} \times P_k\), and Metrophor origin \(O_k \colon P_k \to \mathcal{P}(\mathcal{B})\). * Constructor Signature \(\mathfrak{f}_{\mathrm{ops}}(P)\): Free term algebra \(\{\operatorname{Conj}(X,Y), \operatorname{Lift}_{\BoxSyn}(X), \operatorname{ParaConj}(X)\}\) with immediate predecessor relation \(I_{\mathrm{term}} \subset P \times \mathfrak{f}_{\mathrm{ops}}(P)\). * Level-Shift Functor \(\Shift \colon \CSL \to \CSL\): Maps \(\Shift(\mathbf{L})_k \coloneqq \mathbf{L}_{k+1}\) and \((\Shift(g))_k \coloneqq g_{k+1}\).

Proposition 2.14 (Functoriality of \(\Shift\)) & 3-Step Proof: \(\Shift(\operatorname{id}_{\mathbf{L}}) = \operatorname{id}_{\Shift(\mathbf{L})}\) and \(\Shift(h \circ g) = \Shift(h) \circ \Shift(g)\).

Proof: (1) \((\Shift(\operatorname{id}_{\mathbf{L}}))_k = (\operatorname{id}_{\mathbf{L}})_{k+1} = \operatorname{id}_{P_{k+1}}\). (2) \((\Shift(h \circ g))_k = (h \circ g)_{k+1} = h_{k+1} \circ g_{k+1} = (\Shift(h) \circ \Shift(g))_k\). (3) For \((X,Y) \in I_k^{\Shift(\mathbf{L})} = I_{k+1}^{\mathbf{L}}\), \(((g_k(X), g_{k+1}(Y))) \in I_{k+1}^{\mathbf{M}} = I_k^{\Shift(\mathbf{M})}\), preserving IGP, stability, and origin at index \(k+1\). \(\blacksquare\)

Def. 2.15, Lemma 2.16 & Def. 2.17: Free Syntrix \(\mathbf{L}^*\) & Spine \(\Cspine\) Inductively setting \(P_0 = \AP_0\), \(P_{k+1} = \mathfrak{f}_{\mathrm{ops}}(P_k)\), \(S_{k+1}(T) = \bigwedge_{I_{\mathrm{term}}(X,T)} S_k(X)\), and \(O_{k+1}(T) = \bigcup_{I_{\mathrm{term}}(X,T)} O_k(X)\) defines the Free Syntrix \(\mathbf{L}^*\).

  • Lemma 2.16 (Ancestral Preservation under Lift):
    1. \(S_r(X) = 1 \implies S_{r+1}(\operatorname{Lift}_{\BoxSyn}(X)) = 1\).
    2. \(O_{r+1}(\operatorname{Lift}_{\BoxSyn}(X)) = O_r(X)\).
    3. \((X,Y) \in I_r^+ \implies (\operatorname{Lift}_{\BoxSyn}(X), \operatorname{Lift}_{\BoxSyn}(Y)) \in I_{r+1}^+\).
  • Thin Spine Subcategory \(\Cspine \hookrightarrow \CSL\) (Fig. 2.4): Objects \(\mathbf{L}^{(k)} \coloneqq \Shift^k(\mathbf{L}^*)\) with single-step modal lifts \(f_k = \operatorname{Lift}_{\BoxSyn} \colon \mathbf{L}^{(k)} \to \mathbf{L}^{(k+1)}\), isomorphic to the poset \((\mathbb{N}_0, \le)\).
Manim Act VI (Fig. 2.4): The Thin Spine Subcategory C_spine ↪ C_SL & Shift Endofunctor Ladder

Section 2.3 constructs the discrete category of leveled structures C_SL(B), proves the functoriality of the level-shift endofunctor Shift (Proposition 2.14), and isolates the thin spine subcategory C_spine of iterated modal lifts.

§2.4.1: The 4 Elementary Structures, Selection Proof & Running Example

STRATUM B: SELECTION REPRESENTATIONS (DEF. 2.18, THMS. 2.19–2.23, COR. 2.24 & STAGE II EXAMPLE)

Def. 2.18 & Thm. 2.19 (Selection Dimension Theorem & 4-Step Proof): On an \(n\)-dimensional Banach space \(V \in \FinBan\) with ordered basis \(B = \{e_1, \dots, e_n\}\):

  1. \(\mathcal{S}_{(1)}\) Heterometral Symmetric (\(\Sym_{\mathrm{het}}^m(V,B) \cong \Lambda^m V\)): Strict chains \(1 \le i_1 < \dots < i_m \le n \implies \dim = \binom{n}{m}\).
  2. \(\mathcal{S}_{(2)}\) Heterometral Asymmetric (\(V^{\underline{\otimes}m}(B)\)): Pairwise distinct \(m\)-permutations \(\implies \dim = \frac{n!}{(n-m)!} = m!\binom{n}{m}\).
  3. \(\mathcal{S}_{(3)}\) Homometral Symmetric (\(\Sym^m V\)): Degree-\(m\) commuting monomials (stars-and-bars) \(\implies \dim = \binom{n+m-1}{m}\).
  4. \(\mathcal{S}_{(4)}\) Homometral Asymmetric (\(V^{\otimes m}\)): Unrestricted simple tensors \(\implies \dim = n^m\). \(\blacksquare\)

Lemma 2.20, Thms. 2.21–2.23 & Cor. 2.24 (Recursions & Äquisyndromatik): * Lemma 2.20 (\(k\)-Fold Asymmetric Bridge): \(n_1 = k!\binom{m}{k}\binom{n}{m} = \frac{n!}{(m-k)!(n-m)!} = \binom{n}{m-k}\frac{(n-m+k)!}{(n-m)!}\). * Thm 2.23 (Homogeneous & Homometral Recursions): With \(N_\gamma = n + \sum_{j=1}^\gamma n_j\) and effective class \(A \coloneqq m - \sum_{j=1}^L (a_j - 1)\), pyramidal grows as \(\binom{n_\gamma}{A}\) and homogeneous as \(\binom{N_\gamma}{A}\). * Thm 2.21 (Äquisyndromatik): Constant \(n_\gamma = n\) holds iff heterometral \(m = n-1\) or homometral \(A = 1\).

Stage II of VI Running Example (Polarimeter Syndrome Tree): For \(F_0 = \mathbb{R}^6\), binary alternating detection yields \(F_1 = \Lambda^2 F_0\) of \(\dim \binom{6}{2} = 15\) (3 intra-channel + 9 cross-channel pairs), and \(F_2 = \Lambda^2 F_1\) of \(\dim \binom{15}{2} = 105\). Programming arity \(m_3 = 16 > 15\) forces \(\Lambda^{16} F_1 = 0\) (Syndromabschluß).

Def. 2.25 & Prop. 2.26: Komplexsynkolatoren & Syndromabschluß (SM-8) Stepping arity \(m(\gamma) = \sum_{k=1}^K m_k \mathbf{1}_{I_k}(\gamma)\) across interval partitions \(I_k = [\chi(k-1), \chi(k)-1]\) (\(N_{\mathrm{orderings}} = \chi!\), Thm. 2.22): choosing \(m_K > \dim(F_{\chi(K-1)})\) forces \(\Lambda^{m_K}(F_{\chi(K-1)}) = \{0\}\), achieving deterministic Syndromabschluß (\(F_\gamma \cong \{0\}\;\forall \gamma \ge \chi(K)\)).

Theorem 2.27 (Dynamical Phase Trichotomy of \(n_{\gamma+1} = \binom{n_\gamma}{m}\)) & Proof:

  1. Sub-Critical (\(n_0 \le m\)): \(\binom{n_0}{m} = 0\) (for \(n_0 < m\)) or \(\binom{m}{m} = 1 \implies n_2 = 0\).
  2. Critical Fixed Point (\(n_0 = m+1\)): \(\binom{m+1}{m} = m+1 \implies n_\gamma = m+1\;\forall \gamma \ge 0\).
  3. Super-Critical (\(n_0 \ge m+2\)): Since \(\frac{1}{n}\binom{n}{m}\) is strictly increasing for \(n \ge m+1\), \(\binom{n}{m} \ge \frac{m+2}{2}n > n\). Bounding \(\frac{(n-m+1)^m}{m!} \le \binom{n}{m} \le \frac{n^m}{m!}\) shows \(a_\gamma \coloneqq m^{-\gamma}\ln n_\gamma\) satisfies \(|a_{\gamma+1} - a_\gamma| = \mathcal{O}(m^{-\gamma})\), so \((a_\gamma)\) is Cauchy with limit \(\theta(n_0, m) > 0\): \[ n_\gamma \;\sim\; (m!)^{\frac{1}{m-1}} \exp\!\big(\theta(n_0, m)\, m^\gamma\big) \quad (\gamma \to \infty).\;\blacksquare \]

§2.5: Boardman–Vogt Tensor, Beck Distributive Law & BLT Proof

STRATUM B: SCHOLIA 2.28/2.33, BOARDMAN–VOGT QUOTIENT (DEF. 2.30) & BECK PROOF (THM. 2.31)

Why Naive Free-Tree Substitution Fails I (Plotkin 2004; Klin 2013) On raw syntax trees \(\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{pyr}}, \mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{frag}}\), pushing a multi-ary pyramidal node across a fragment node duplicates subtrees; distinct composites encode the same wiring while choosing different representatives, violating Beck’s multiplication pentagons.

Scholia 2.28 & 2.33: Why Beck Distributivity Governs Spaltbarkeit Two monads \(T_1 \circ T_2\) do not automatically form a monad. Quotienting by the Boardman–Vogt interchange ideal \(\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{exchange}}\) induces a natural transformation \(\widehat{\lambda} \colon \overline{T}_{\mathrm{pyr}}\overline{T}_{\mathrm{frag}} \Rightarrow \overline{T}_{\mathrm{frag}}\overline{T}_{\mathrm{pyr}}\) satisfying Beck’s 4 coherence diagrams (1969).

Theorem 2.31 (Beck Distributive Law on Quotient Tree Monads) & BLT Proof: Every \(t \in \overline{T}_{\mathrm{pyr}}(\overline{T}_{\mathrm{frag}} X)\) is the limit of a Cauchy sequence of finite tree classes \([t_k]\) under the \(\ell^1\)-projective quotient norm. Because \(\lambda_X\) acts on representatives by the isometric Boardman–Vogt block permutation \(\sigma_{n,m} \in \Sigma_{nm}\) and contractive substitutions (\(\|\circ_i\|_{\op} \le 1\)), \(\|\lambda_X([t_k])\| \le \|[t_k]\|\). By the Bounded Linear Transformation (BLT) Theorem, \(\lambda_X\) extends uniquely to \(\widehat{\lambda}_X\) with \(\|\widehat{\lambda}_X\|_{\op} \le 1\), and \(T_{\mathrm{hom}} \coloneqq \overline{T}_{\mathrm{frag}} \circ \overline{T}_{\mathrm{pyr}}\) is a composite monad! \(\blacksquare\)

Interactive 4-Tab Inspector: Spaltbarkeit & Beck Coherence Laws

1. Operadic Spaltbarkeit via Boardman–Vogt Tensor Product (Def. 2.30) \[ \Ohom \coloneqq \Opyr \otBV \Ofrag \coloneqq \frac{\Opyr \amalg \Ofrag}{\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{exchange}}}, \qquad f \circ (g, \dots, g) \;\sim\; \sigma_{n,m} \cdot \big( g \circ (f, \dots, f) \big) \] By universal property, an \(\Ohom\)-algebra is canonically an \(\Opyr\)-algebra internal to \(\operatorname{Alg}_{\Ofrag}(\Ban)\): \[ \operatorname{Alg}_{\Ohom}(\Ban) \;\cong\; \operatorname{Alg}_{\Opyr}\!\Big( \operatorname{Alg}_{\Ofrag}(\Ban) \Big) \]

2. Recursive Presentation of the Interchange Transformation \(\lambda_X\) (Prop. 2.32) * Unit Base Case: \(\lambda_X(\eta_{\mathrm{pyr}}(u)) = \overline{T}_{\mathrm{frag}}(\eta_{\mathrm{pyr}} X)(u)\) for all \(u \in \overline{T}_{\mathrm{frag}} X\). * Interchange Distribution: For \(\psi \in \Opyr(n)\) and \(u_1, \dots, u_n \in \overline{T}_{\mathrm{frag}} X\): \[ \lambda_X\big(\psi(u_1, \dots, u_n)\big) \;=\; \Big[ \mu_{\mathrm{frag}} \circ \overline{T}_{\mathrm{frag}}(\psi)\big(\lambda_X(u_1), \dots, \lambda_X(u_n)\big) \Big]_{\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{exchange}}} \] Pulls historical cross-level interactions (\(\overline{T}_{\mathrm{frag}}\)) to the outer layer while keeping step-by-step deduction (\(\overline{T}_{\mathrm{pyr}}\)) in the inner core!

3. Beck Multiplication Pentagon & Composite Multiplication \(\mu_{\mathrm{hom}}\) (Def. 2.34, Fig. 2.7A) \[ \mu_{\mathrm{hom}} \;\coloneqq\; (\mu_{\mathrm{frag}} \overline{T}_{\mathrm{pyr}}) \circ (\overline{T}_{\mathrm{frag}} \overline{T}_{\mathrm{frag}} \mu_{\mathrm{pyr}}) \circ (\overline{T}_{\mathrm{frag}} \widehat{\lambda} \overline{T}_{\mathrm{pyr}}) \] Satisfies the two Beck multiplication pentagons (mult_pyr and mult_frag in Syntrometrie.StratumA.Operads.BeckDistributiveLaw): \[ (\mu_{\mathrm{pyr}} \overline{T}_{\mathrm{frag}}) \Rightarrow \widehat{\lambda} \;=\; (\overline{T}_{\mathrm{pyr}}\widehat{\lambda}) \Rightarrow (\widehat{\lambda}\overline{T}_{\mathrm{pyr}}) \Rightarrow (\overline{T}_{\mathrm{frag}}\mu_{\mathrm{pyr}}) \]

4. Beck Unit Triangles & Composite Unit \(\eta_{\mathrm{hom}}\) (Def. 2.34, Fig. 2.7B) \[ \eta_{\mathrm{hom}} \;\coloneqq\; (\eta_{\mathrm{frag}} \overline{T}_{\mathrm{pyr}}) \circ \eta_{\mathrm{pyr}} \colon \operatorname{Id}_{\Ban} \Longrightarrow \overline{T}_{\mathrm{frag}} \circ \overline{T}_{\mathrm{pyr}} \] Satisfies both unit compatibility triangles (unit_pyr and unit_frag): \[ \widehat{\lambda} \circ (\eta_{\mathrm{pyr}} \overline{T}_{\mathrm{frag}}) = \overline{T}_{\mathrm{frag}} \eta_{\mathrm{pyr}}, \qquad \widehat{\lambda} \circ (\overline{T}_{\mathrm{pyr}} \eta_{\mathrm{frag}}) = \eta_{\mathrm{frag}} \overline{T}_{\mathrm{pyr}} \]

1. Hereditary Semantics of \(\BoxSyn \phi\) & Demarcation from \(\Box_S \phi\) (Rem. 2.3) * Base (\(a_i \in P_0 = \AP_0\)): \((U, k) \models \BoxSyn a_i \iff a_i \in \AP_0 \wedge (U, k) \models a_i\). * Inductive (\(\phi \in P_{p+1},\; p+1 \le k\)): \[ (U, k) \models \BoxSyn \phi \;\Longleftrightarrow\; (U, k) \models \phi \;\wedge\; \forall X \in P_p\big((X, \phi) \in I_{p+1} \implies (U, k) \models \BoxSyn X\big) \] * Semantic Demarcation: \(\Box_S \phi\) is horizontal aspect necessity across \(R_{\Box_S}\)-close contexts; \(\BoxSyn \phi\) is vertical hereditary grounding in the Metrophor \(\AP_0\).

2. Syntrix-Internal Stability Introduction Rules (\(\BoxSyn\mathfrak{a}I, \BoxSyn FI\)) \[ \frac{a_i \in \AP_0 \qquad S(x); \Gamma \vdash^0 a_i}{S(x); \Gamma \vdash^0 \BoxSyn a_i}\;(\BoxSyn\mathfrak{a}I) \] \[ \frac{S(x); \Gamma \vdash^{j+1} \phi' \qquad \forall X \in P_j\,\big((X, \phi') \in I_{j+1} \to S(x); \Gamma \vdash^j \BoxSyn X\big)}{S(x); \Gamma \vdash^{j+1} \BoxSyn \phi'}\;(\BoxSyn FI) \]

3. Syntrix-Internal Stability Elimination Rules (\(\BoxSyn E_T, \BoxSyn E_{\mathrm{IGP}}\)) \[ \frac{S(x); \Gamma \vdash^j \BoxSyn \phi}{S(x); \Gamma \vdash^j \phi}\;(\BoxSyn E_T) \qquad\qquad \frac{S(x); \Gamma \vdash^{j+1} \BoxSyn \phi' \qquad ((X, \phi') \in I_{j+1})}{S(x); \Gamma \vdash^j \BoxSyn X}\;(\BoxSyn E_{\mathrm{IGP}}) \]

4. Def. 2.35, Theorem 2.36 (Spine Reflexivity \(\rho^{(n)}\)) & Full Proof (Fig. 2.8) Define \((\rho_k^{(n)})_j(X) \coloneqq \operatorname{Lift}_{\BoxSyn}^n(X)\) for \(X \in P_{j+k}\).

Proof of Naturality & \((\mathbb{N}_0,+)\)-Coherence:

  1. Naturality Square: Evaluating both paths on \(X \in P_{j+k}\) in the free term algebra \(\mathfrak{f}_{\mathrm{ops}}\): \[ \begin{aligned} (\Shift^n(f_k) \circ \rho_k^{(n)})_j(X) &= (f_{k+n})_j\big(\operatorname{Lift}_{\BoxSyn}^n(X)\big) = \operatorname{Lift}_{\BoxSyn}^{n+1}(X) \\ (\rho_{k+1}^{(n)} \circ f_k)_j(X) &= (\rho_{k+1}^{(n)})_j\big(\operatorname{Lift}_{\BoxSyn}(X)\big) = \operatorname{Lift}_{\BoxSyn}^{n+1}(X) \end{aligned} \] By Lemma 2.16, stability \(S_{j+k}(X)=1\) and origin \(O_{j+k}(X)\) are preserved.
  2. Graded Reflection Law: \((\rho_{k+n}^{(m)} \circ \rho_k^{(n)})_j(X) = \operatorname{Lift}_{\BoxSyn}^m(\operatorname{Lift}_{\BoxSyn}^n(X)) = \operatorname{Lift}_{\BoxSyn}^{n+m}(X) = (\rho_k^{(n+m)})_j(X)\). \(\blacksquare\)

import Mathlib
open CategoryTheory
namespace Syntrometrie.StratumA.Operads

class BeckDistributiveLaw {C : Type*} [Category C]
    (T_pyr T_frag : Monad C) where
  dist : (T_pyr.toFunctor ⋙ T_frag.toFunctor) ⟶
         (T_frag.toFunctor ⋙ T_pyr.toFunctor)
  unit_pyr : (myWhiskerRight T_pyr.η T_frag.toFunctor) ≫ dist =
             myWhiskerLeft T_frag.toFunctor T_pyr.η
  unit_frag : (myWhiskerLeft T_pyr.toFunctor T_frag.η) ≫ dist =
              myWhiskerRight T_frag.η T_pyr.toFunctor
  mult_pyr : (myWhiskerRight T_pyr.μ T_frag.toFunctor) ≫ dist =
             (myWhiskerLeft T_pyr.toFunctor dist) ≫
             (myWhiskerRight dist T_pyr.toFunctor) ≫
             (myWhiskerLeft T_frag.toFunctor T_pyr.μ)

  Nat.choose_succ_self_right m

§2.10–2.11: Sanity Checks, Deliverables & Bridge to Chapter 3
