STRATUM D: METROPLEXTOTALITÄT (T₁..Tₙ), FUNCTOR HIERARCHY S(n+1) & SM EQS. 21–24

Metroplextotalität (\(T_1\)), Protosimplexe & Functor Hierarchy \(S(n+1)\) (SM, pp. 85–88): * Syntrixtotalität (\(T0\)) vs. Metroplextotalität 1. Grades (\(T_1\)): \(T_1\) is the totality of all realizations of Hypersyntrices and their corporate combinations (Hypertotalitäten & Enyphanmetroplexe). * Generative Functor Hierarchy: * \(S1 \equiv \mathfrak{f}\): Generates grade-0 Syntrices (\(\overset{\scriptscriptstyle 0}{\mathbf{M}}\)) from \(\mathbf{AP}_0\). * \(S2 \equiv {}^1\mathfrak{f}\): Generates grade-1 Hypersyntrices (\(\overset{\scriptscriptstyle 1}{\mathbf{M}}\)) from \(\overset{\scriptscriptstyle 0}{\mathbf{M}}\). * \(S(n+1) \equiv {}^n\mathfrak{f}\): Generates grade-\(n\) Metroplexe (\(\overset{\scriptscriptstyle n}{\mathbf{M}}\)) from grade-\((n-1)\) Metroplexe. * Protosimplexe (SM, p. 87): Minimal, irreducible structural units within a state totality \(T_n\) serving as unanalyzable atomic objects at the next level.

Recursive Grade-\(n\) Hierarchy & Elementary Basis (SM Eqs. 21–24, pp. 89–92): \[ \widetilde{\mathrm{a}} \equiv \overset{\scriptscriptstyle 0}{\mathbf{M}}\widetilde{\mathrm{a}} \;\;(\text{SM-21}), \qquad \widetilde{\mathrm{a}}_i \equiv \widetilde{\mathrm{p}}_{(k)i} \;\;(\text{SM-22}), \qquad \overset{\scriptscriptstyle 1}{\mathbf{M}}\widetilde{\mathrm{a}},\; \overline{|B|},\; \mathrm{C},\; \overset{\scriptscriptstyle 1}{\mathbf{M}}\widetilde{\mathrm{p}}_{(k)} \;\;(1 \le k \le 4,\;\text{SM-23}) \] \[ \overset{\scriptscriptstyle n+1}{\mathbf{M}}\widetilde{\mathrm{a}} \;\equiv\; \big\langle \overset{\scriptscriptstyle n}{\mathbf{M}}\widetilde{\mathrm{F}},\, \overset{\scriptscriptstyle\sim}{\mathbf{M}}{}^n\widetilde{\mathrm{a}}\,\underline{\mathrm{r}} \big\rangle \quad \lor \quad \overset{\scriptscriptstyle\sim}{\mathbf{M}}{}^n\widetilde{\mathrm{a}} \;\equiv\; \left(\overset{\scriptscriptstyle n}{\mathbf{M}}\widetilde{\mathrm{a}}_{(p)}\right)_{N_n} \tag{SM-24} \]

Duale Endogene Tektonik & Kontraktion (SM, p. 93): 1. Graduelle Tektonik (Vertical / Grade Tectonics): The vertical, nested compositional hierarchy of the Metroplex across its associated lower grades \(0 \le k \le n-1\). 2. Syndromatische Tektonik (Horizontal / Syndrome Tectonics): The horizontal, sequential organization of syndrome layers generated within a given grade by the active synkolators \({}^k\mathfrak{f}\). 3. Kontraktion (\(\kappa \colon \overset{\scriptscriptstyle n}{\mathbf{M}} \to \overset{\scriptscriptstyle m}{\mathbf{M}}',\; m < n\)): Formally compresses a high-grade Metroplex into an effective lower-grade representation while preserving dominant structural invariants.

Manim Act II: Generative Functor Hierarchy S(n+1), SM-21..24 & Duale Endogene Tektonik

§5.1.4–5.1.5: Syntrokline Metroplexbrücken & Metroplexdiabatik

STRATUM D: SYNTROKLINE BRÜCKEN (SM EQS. 25, 25a), EN-KOMBINATION (SM-26) & RÜCKKOPPELUNG

Syntrokline Metroplexbrücken (SM Eqs. 25, 25a, pp. 94–98): Totalities \(T_n\) act as Etagen (floors); bridges \({}^{n+N}\alpha\) act as Aufzüge (elevators) lifting lower-grade structures across \(N\) intervening grades: \[ \overset{\scriptscriptstyle n+N}{\tilde{\alpha}} \;\equiv\; \underset{n}{\overset{n+N}{\mathrm{S}}}\, \overset{\scriptscriptstyle n}{\mathbf{M}}\widetilde{\mathrm{a}} \Big[ \big(\overset{\scriptscriptstyle n-1}{\widetilde{\Phi}}_{j(n)\gamma}\big) \big(\overset{\scriptscriptstyle n-1}{\widetilde{\mathrm{f}}}_{j(n)\gamma}, \underline{\mathrm{m}}_{j(n)\gamma}\big) \big(\overset{\scriptscriptstyle n}{\widetilde{\mathrm{f}}}_\gamma, \underline{\mathrm{r}}_\gamma\big) \big(\overset{\scriptscriptstyle n+1}{\widetilde{\mathrm{f}}}, \mathrm{p}\big) \Big]_{\gamma=1}^{k_{(n)}} \tag{SM-25a} \] Endogene Kombinationen (SM Eq. 26, p. 102): \[ \overset{\scriptscriptstyle\sim}{\mathbf{M}}{}^n \;\equiv\; \overset{\scriptscriptstyle n}{\mathbf{M}}\widetilde{\mathrm{a}} \,\mathrm{EN}\, \overset{\scriptscriptstyle p+q}{\mathbf{M}}\widetilde{\mathrm{b}} \qquad (p + q \le n \;\land\; q > 0) \tag{SM-26} \]

Remark (Conceptual Preamble: Why Higher Scale Hierarchies Require an \((\infty,1)\)-Topos): Following Marquis (2009), Zalamea (2012), and Lurie (2009), when a system of Syntrices is bundled into a higher-grade Metroplex, corporate transformations become 1-morphisms, transformations between those become 2-morphisms, and so on ad infinitum. In strict 1-category theory, forcing multi-scale identifications to hold as rigid point-set equalities causes the hierarchy to collapse into inconsistency. In an \((\infty,1)\)-topos, composition is governed by coherent higher homotopies rather than brittle strict equality.

Interactive Syntrokline Bridge & Re-Entrant Diabatik Simulator

Context (§5.1.4–5.1.5, SM p. 103): Toggle between Inward Metrophoric Diabatik (\(\gamma = 0\)) (external coupling alters the Metroplex Idea), Outward Syndromatic Diabatik (\(\gamma > 0\)) (internal change emits coupling outward), and Closed Syntrokline Rückkoppelung (re-entrant feedback loop across floors \(T_{m-1} \leftrightarrow T_m\)).

Manim Act III: Syntrokline Metroplexbrücken (SM-25a), EN-Product (SM-26) & Closed Re-Entrant Feedback

§5.2.1: Ambient \((\infty, 1)\)-Topos \(\Xsyn\) & Universe Stratification

STRATUM A: META-THEORETIC JUSTIFICATION, DEF. 5.1 & CUMULATIVE UNIVERSES (FIG. 5.2)

Scholium (Meta-Theoretic Justification: Ambient \((\infty,1)\)-Topos & Dendroidal Segal Objects): Why formulate the Metroplex hierarchy inside a single ambient \((\infty, 1)\)-topos \(\Xsyn = \mathbf{Sh}_\infty(\Ctx, \Jsyn)\) rather than iterating sites \(\mathbf{Site}_{n+1} = \mathbf{Sh}(\mathbf{Site}_n)\)? 1. Universe Safety (\(\mathcal{U}_0 \in \mathcal{U}_1 \in \mathcal{U}_2\)): Iterated site-building explodes set-theoretic universes at every step. Housing all grades inside a single \(\mathcal{U}_0\)-presentable \((\infty,1)\)-topos ensures every Metroplex \(\mathbf{M}^n\) remains \(\mathcal{U}_0\)-small inside \(\Xsyn\). 2. Dendroidal Complete Segal Objects (\(\dCSeg(\Xsyn)\)): Because synkolators \(f \in \Osyn(m)\) have multi-ary valence \(m \ge 1\), grade-1 Hypersyntrices are modeled over the Moerdijk–Weiss tree category \(\mathbf{\Omega}\), avoiding strict associativity pitfalls via Rezk’s completeness condition. 3. Adjoint Syntroclinic Bridges: Change-of-operad adjunctions \((\phi_{n, n+N})_! \dashv (\phi_{n, n+N})^*\) replace ad-hoc cross-scale mappings.

Definition 5.1: Ambient \((\infty, 1)\)-Topos \(\Xsyn\) & Postnikov Tower Fix Grothendieck universes \(\mathcal{U}_0 \in \mathcal{U}_1 \in \mathcal{U}_2\) and \(\mathcal{S} \coloneqq \mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{U}_0}\). The Ambient Syntrometric \((\infty, 1)\)-Topos is the left-exact topological localization: \[ \Xsyn \;\coloneqq\; \mathbf{Sh}_\infty\big(\Ctx, \Jsyn\big) \;\coloneqq\; L_{\Jsyn}\!\Big(\operatorname{Fun}\big((\Ctx)^{\mathrm{op}}, \mathcal{S}\big)\Big) \] 1. 0-Truncated Recovery: \(\tau_{\le 0}\Xsyn \simeq \mathbf{Sh}(\Ctx, \Jsyn) = \Esyn\) recovers the 1-topos of Chapter 1. 2. Higher Homotopy Truncation: \(\tau_{\le m} \colon \Xsyn \to \Xsyn^{\le m}\) is left adjoint to the inclusion \(\iota_m \colon \Xsyn^{\le m} \hookrightarrow \Xsyn\).

Size Discipline & Cumulative Universe Stratification (Fig. 5.2): * \(\mathcal{U}_0\) (Internal): Small site \(\Ctx\), objects \(X \in \operatorname{Obj}(\Xsyn)\), internal Metroplexe \(\mathbf{M}^n \in \operatorname{Obj}(\Met_n(\Xsyn))\). * \(\mathcal{U}_1\) (Ambient): Space \(\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{U}_0}\), topos \(\Xsyn\), categories \(\Met_n(\Xsyn)\) and \(\Alg_{\On}(\Xsyn)\) (\(\mathcal{U}_0\)-presentable, locally \(\mathcal{U}_0\)-small). * \(\mathcal{U}_2\) (Meta-Slice): External functor categories \(\operatorname{Fun}(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}, \Met_n(\Xsyn))\), external slice totalities \(T_n^{\mathrm{ext}}\), or enlarged \(\Xsyn^{(k)} \coloneqq \mathbf{Sh}_\infty(\Ctx, \Jsyn; \mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{U}_k})\).

Manim Act IV (Fig. 5.2): Cumulative Universe Stratification U₀ ∈ U₁ ∈ U₂ & Size Invariance in X_syn

§5.2.2–5.2.3: Dendroidal Complete Segal Spaces & Grafting Proof

STRATUM A/B: DENDROIDAL SEGAL SPACES (DEF. 5.2), GRAFTING PROOF (PROP. 5.3) & OPERAD AXIOM (ASSUMP. 5.4)

Definition 5.2: Dendroidal Complete Segal Objects \(\dCSeg(\Xsyn)\) Let \(\mathbf{\Omega}\) be the Moerdijk–Weiss dendroidal category of finite rooted trees (\(i \colon \mathbf{\Delta} \hookrightarrow \mathbf{\Omega}\) embedding linear trees \([k]\)). A dendroidal object \(X \colon \mathbf{\Omega}^{\mathrm{op}} \to \Xsyn\) is a Dendroidal Complete Segal Space (\(X \in \dCSeg(\Xsyn)\)) if: 1. Dendroidal Segal Condition: For every tree \(T \in \mathbf{\Omega}\) with vertices \(V(T)\) and \(n(v)\)-corollas \(C_{n(v)} \hookrightarrow T\) glued along inner edges \(E_{\mathrm{int}}(T)\): \[ X(T) \;\xrightarrow{\;\sim\;}\; \lim_{v \in V(T)}^{\Xsyn} X(C_{n(v)}) \;\simeq\; X(C_{n(v_1)}) \mathop{\times}\limits^{\mathrm{h}}_{X(\eta_{e_1})} \cdots \mathop{\times}\limits^{\mathrm{h}}_{X(\eta_{e_k})} X(C_{n(v_{k+1})}) \] 2. Rezk Completeness: \((i^*X)_0 \xrightarrow{\sim} (i^*X)_{\mathrm{hoequiv}} \subset (i^*X)_1\).

Proposition 5.3 & Proof (Explicit Dendroidal Segal Grafting for Binary Hypersyntrices): Let \(T = C_2^{(v)} \circ_e C_2^{(u)} \in \operatorname{Obj}(\mathbf{\Omega})\) graft two binary 2-corollas along inner edge \(\eta_e\). 1. Grafting Pullback: The dendroidal spine is the pushout \(\operatorname{Sc}[T] = C_2^{(v)} \amalg_{\eta_e} C_2^{(u)}\) in \(\mathbf{\Omega}\), mapping to the homotopy pullback \(X(T) \xrightarrow{\sim} X(C_2^{(v)}) \times_{X(\eta_e)}^{\mathrm{h}} X(C_2^{(u)})\) in \(\Xsyn\). 2. Ternary Composite \(\partial_{\eta_e}^* \colon X(T) \to X(C_3)\): Contracting \(\eta_e\) gives the inner face \(\partial_{\eta_e} \colon C_3 \hookrightarrow T\). Inverting the Segal equivalence up to homotopy and applying \(\partial_{\eta_e}^*\) composes compatible binary operations \((v, u) \mapsto v \circ_e u \in X(C_3)\). 3. Associativity Homotopy: On a 3-vertex tree \(T_3\) with inner edges \(e_1, e_2\), commuting face maps \(\partial_{e_1}\partial_{e_2} = \partial_{e_2}\partial_{e_1} \colon C_4 \hookrightarrow T_3\) induce \((w \circ_1 v) \circ_1 u \simeq w \circ_1 (v \circ_2 u)\) inside \(X(C_4)\). \(\blacksquare\)

Interactive Dendroidal Corolla Grafting & Segal Spine Explorer (Prop. 5.3)

Context (§5.2.2, Prop. 5.3): Step through the dendroidal Segal condition on the grafted binary tree \(T = C_2^{(v)} \circ_e C_2^{(u)}\), the inner-edge contraction \(\partial_{\eta_e}\) yielding the composite ternary corolla \(C_3\), and the 3-vertex associativity homotopy.

Assumption 5.4: The Metroplex Operadic Realization Axiom (\(\{\On\}_{n \ge 0}\)) There exists an inductive family of internal \((\infty, 1)\)-operads \(\{\On\}_{n \ge 0}\) in \(\Xsyn\) (\(\mathcal{O}_0 \coloneqq N_\Delta(\Osyn)\)) with: 1. Free-Forgetful Monad Adjunction: \(\Free_{\On} \colon \Xsyn \rightleftarrows \Alg_{\On}(\Xsyn) \colon U_n\) (\(T_{\On} = U_n \circ \Free_{\On}\)). 2. Change-of-Operad Adjunctions: Operad morphisms \(\phi_{n, n+1} \colon \On \to \mathcal{O}_{n+1}\) induce \((\phi_{n, n+1})_! \colon \Alg_{\On}(\Xsyn) \rightleftarrows \Alg_{\mathcal{O}_{n+1}}(\Xsyn) \colon (\phi_{n, n+1})^*\), with \(\phi_{n, n+N} \coloneqq \phi_{n+N-1, n+N} \circ \dots \circ \phi_{n, n+1}\).

Manim Act V: Dendroidal Complete Segal Spaces dCSeg(X_syn), Corolla Grafting & Operads {O_n}

§5.3.1: Uniform Metroplex Categories \(\Met_n(\Xsyn)\) & Toy Example

STRATUM B: UNIFORM METROPLEX CATEGORIES (DEFS. 5.5–5.6), TOY EXAMPLE & 3D TRANSCENDENCE TOWER (FIG. 5.1)

Definition 5.5: Uniform Categories of Grade-\(n\) Metroplexe \(\Met_n(\Xsyn)\) For every grade \(n \ge 0\), \(\Met_n(\Xsyn)\) is a full \((\infty, 1)\)-subcategory of \(\Alg_{\On}(\Xsyn)\): 1. Grade \(n = 0\) (Base Syntrices): \(\Met_0(\Xsyn) \coloneqq \Alg_{\mathcal{O}_0}(\Xsyn)\). 2. Grade \(n = 1\) (Hypersyntrices): \(\Met_1(\Xsyn) \simeq \dCSeg(\Xsyn) \hookrightarrow \Alg_{\mathcal{O}_1}(\Xsyn)\) (with linear spine \(i^*\Met_1(\Xsyn) = \CSeg(\Xsyn) \simeq \operatorname{Cat}_\infty(\Xsyn)\)). 3. Grade \(n \ge 2\) (Higher Metroplexe): \(\Met_n(\Xsyn) \subseteq \Alg_{\On}(\Xsyn)\) generated by hypermetrophoric colimits of grade-\((n-1)\) diagrams. Equipped with conservative forgetful functors \(U_n^{\Met} \colon \Met_n(\Xsyn) \to \Xsyn\).

Definition 5.6: Hypermetrophors as Simplicial Diagrams \(H_n\) For \(n \ge 1\), a Grade-\(n\) Hypermetrophor is a simplicial diagram \(H_n \colon \mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}} \to \Met_{n-1}(\Xsyn)\) whose vertices \([0] \mapsto H_{n,0}\) are grade-\((n-1)\) Metroplexe and whose 1-simplices \([1] \mapsto H_{n,1}\) encode corporate pairings (\(\mathbf{Kor}_n\)). Its geometric realization colimit is: \[ \HypMet_n \;\coloneqq\; \colim_{[k] \in \mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}} \big( U_{n-1}^{\Met} \circ H_n([k]) \big) \;\in\; \Xsyn, \qquad \overset{\scriptscriptstyle n}{\mathbf{M}}\mathcal{S} \;\coloneqq\; \Free_{\On}(\HypMet_n) \]

Remark (Toy Example: The Hypersyntrix \(\overset{\scriptscriptstyle 1}{\mathbf{M}}\) as an Internal 2-Object Category): Consider two base Syntrices \(\mathcal{S}_1, \mathcal{S}_2 \in \Met_0(\Xsyn)\) coupled by a corporate pairing \(\mathbf{Kor}(\mathcal{S}_1, \mathcal{S}_2)\). How do they form a grade-1 Hypersyntrix \(\overset{\scriptscriptstyle 1}{\mathbf{M}} \in \CSeg(\Xsyn)\)? * Object Space (\([0]\)): \(C_0 \coloneqq \mathcal{S}_1 \sqcup \mathcal{S}_2\) (the Hypermetrophor \({}^1\mathbf{w}\widetilde{\mathbf{a}}\)). Every entire lower-level Syntrix becomes a single point (0-simplex) at grade 1! * Morphism Space (\([1]\)): \(C_1 \coloneqq \mathbf{Kor}(\mathcal{S}_1, \mathcal{S}_2) \sqcup \operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{S}_1} \sqcup \operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{S}_2}\) (the Metroplexsynkolator \({}^1\mathfrak{f}\)). * Segal Composition (\([2]\)): \(C_2 \cong C_1 \times_{C_0} C_1\), ensuring sequential corporate couplings compose associatively up to coherent homotopy.

Manim Act VI (Fig. 5.1): 3D Transcendence Tower (T₀, T₁, Tₙ), Hypermetrophors Hₙ & Toy Internal Category

§5.3.2–5.3.3: Syntroclinic Bridge Proof & Postnikov Contraction \(\tau_{\le m}\)

STRATUM B: BRIDGE LIFTING PROOF (DEF. 5.7, PROP. 5.8, FIG. 5.3) & POSTNIKOV CONTRACTION (DEF. 5.9, PROP. 5.10, FIG. 5.4)

Definition 5.7 & Proposition 5.8: Syntroclinic Bridge Adjunction For grades \(n \ge 0\) and \(N \ge 1\), the Syntroclinic Bridge is the left adjoint extension-of-scalars functor: \[ B_{n, n+N} \;\coloneqq\; (\phi_{n, n+N})_! \colon \Alg_{\On}(\Xsyn) \;\rightleftarrows\; \Alg_{\mathcal{O}_{n+N}}(\Xsyn) \;\colon (\phi_{n, n+N})^* =: R_{n+N, n} \] On the subcategory \(\mathcal{I}_n \subseteq \Met_n(\Xsyn)\) of invariant syndromes, the restricted bridge \(B_{n, n+N}|_{\mathcal{I}_n}\) is fully faithful if and only if for every \(X \in \operatorname{Obj}(\mathcal{I}_n)\), the adjunction unit is an equivalence in \(\Met_n(\Xsyn)\): \[ \eta_X \colon X \;\xrightarrow{\;\sim\;}\; R_{n+N, n}\big(B_{n, n+N}(X)\big) \]

Proof of Proposition 5.8 (Standard Adjunction Criterion for Fully Faithful Lifting): For any \(X, Y \in \operatorname{Obj}(\mathcal{I}_n)\), the adjunction \(B_{n, n+N} \dashv R_{n+N, n}\) provides a natural homotopy equivalence of mapping spaces: \[ \Map_{\Alg_{\mathcal{O}_{n+N}}}\!\big(B_{n, n+N}(X),\, B_{n, n+N}(Y)\big) \;\simeq\; \Map_{\Alg_{\On}}\!\big(X,\, R_{n+N, n}(B_{n, n+N}(Y))\big) \] Under this equivalence, the action of \(B_{n, n+N}\) on mapping spaces \(\Map(X, Y) \to \Map(B(X), B(Y))\) factors as post-composition with the unit morphism \((\eta_Y)_* \colon \Map(X, Y) \to \Map(X, R(B(Y)))\). By the Yoneda Lemma in \((\infty, 1)\)-categories (Lurie 2009, §5.1.3), \((\eta_Y)_*\) is a homotopy equivalence for all \(X \in \mathcal{I}_n\) iff \(\eta_Y \colon Y \xrightarrow{\sim} R_{n+N, n}(B_{n, n+N}(Y))\) is an equivalence. \(\blacksquare\)

Interactive Postnikov Contraction κ_model := τ_{≤m} ⊣ ι_m (Def. 5.9, Prop. 5.10)

Context (§5.3.3 & Fig. 5.4): Note that homotopy truncation degree \(m\) is distinct from Metroplex grade \(n\). Slide \(m\) to strip away higher homotopy fluctuations (\(\underline{\pi}_k(\tau_{\le m}X) = 0\) for \(k > m\)) while universally preserving \(\Map_{\Xsyn}(\tau_{\le m}X, Y) \simeq \Map_{\Xsyn}(X, Y)\) for all \(m\)-truncated objects \(Y \in \Xsyn^{\le m}\).

Truncation Degree m: m = ∞

§5.3.4: Bisimplicial Tectonic Grid & Homological Banach Realization \(\RBan\)

STRATUM B: BISIMPLICIAL TECTONICS (DEF. 5.11, FIG. 5.5), DOLD–KAN REALIZATION (DEF. 5.12) & THM. 5.13

Interactive 4 × 4 Bisimplicial Tectonic Grid Tect(S)_{p,q} (Fig. 5.5)

Context (Def. 5.11 & Thm. 5.13): Click between the horizontal Syndromatische Tektonik (\(\operatorname{Syn}_\bullet, d_i^h\)), vertical Graduelle Tektonik (\(\operatorname{Gr}_\bullet, d_j^v\)), and the Diagonal Simplicial Realization \(\operatorname{diag}^*\Tect(\mathcal{S})\).

Manim Act VIII (Fig. 5.5): Bisimplicial Tectonic Grid & Homological Banach Realization R_Ban

Definition 5.11: Bisimplicial Tectonic Object \(\Tect(\mathcal{S})\) For an associative Metroplex \(\mathcal{S} \in \Met_n(\Xsyn)\), its dual endogenous architecture is realized as a bisimplicial object: \[ \Tect(\mathcal{S}) \;\in\; \operatorname{Fun}\big(\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}} \times \mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}},\; \Xsyn\big) \] with commuting vertical face/degeneracy operators (\(d_i^v, s_i^v\) on \(\operatorname{Gr}_\bullet\), grades \(0 \le k \le n-1\)) and horizontal operators (\(d_j^h, s_j^h\) on \(\operatorname{Syn}_\bullet\), syndrome layers).

Definition 5.12: Homological Banach Realization Functor \(\RBan\) On \(\Xsyn^{\mathrm{coord}} \subseteq \Xsyn\) (sheaves \(X\) with finitely generated total homology of \(\Gamma(X)\) and basis \(B_X\)), via free \(\mathbb{R}\)-linearization \(\mathbb{R}[-] \colon \mathcal{S} \to s\mathbf{Vect}_{\mathbb{R}}\) and Dold–Kan normalization \(N_\bullet \colon s\mathbf{Vect}_{\mathbb{R}} \xrightarrow{\sim} \mathbf{Ch}_{\ge 0}(\mathbf{Vect}_{\mathbb{R}})\): \[ \RBan(X) \;\coloneqq\; \bigoplus_{k=0}^\infty H_k\!\Big( N_\bullet\big(\mathbb{R}[\Gamma(X)]\big) \Big)_{\ell^1} \;\in\; \operatorname{Obj}(\FinBan) \] Degree \(k=0\) recovers \(H_0 \cong \mathbb{R}[\pi_0(\Gamma(X))]\), while \(k \ge 1\) preserves higher homotopical invariants, yielding \(\Tect_{\mathbf{Ban}}(\mathcal{S}) \coloneqq \RBan \circ \Tect(\mathcal{S})\).

Theorem 5.13: Tectonic 2-Fold Segal Realization Theorem Pre-composing with \(\operatorname{diag} \colon \mathbf{\Delta} \to \mathbf{\Delta} \times \mathbf{\Delta}\) (\([k] \mapsto \Tect(\mathcal{S})_{k,k}\)) yields the Total Tectonic Object: \[ \big|\Tect(\mathcal{S})\big|_{\mathbf{\Delta}} \;\coloneqq\; \colim_{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}}\big(\operatorname{diag}^* \Tect(\mathcal{S})\big) \;\simeq\; \colim_{\mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}} \times \mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}}} \Tect(\mathcal{S})_{p,q} \] unifying vertical gradual and horizontal syndromatic architectures into a single coherent homotopy type in \(\Xsyn\).

§5.3.5: Lawvere–Schreiber Cohesion & The Tectonic Spectral Sequence

STRATUM B: COHESIVE MODALITIES (DEF. 5.14), SPECTRAL SEQUENCE PROOF (THM. 5.15) & CARNAP 5.1

Interactive 4-Tab Inspector: Cohesive \((\infty,1)\)-Topos Modalities (\(\int \dashv \flat \dashv \sharp\))

Context (§5.3.5, Def. 5.14): Inspect Lawvere–Schreiber cohesion (\(\Pi \dashv \Disc \dashv \Gamma \dashv \Codisc\)) uniting discrete combinatorial Enyphanmetroplexe with smooth Äondynen.

1. Homological Banach Realization \(\RBan\) (Def. 5.12) Applies Dold–Kan normalization in both simplicial directions of \(\Tect_{\mathbf{Ban}}(\mathcal{S}) \colon \mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}} \times \mathbf{\Delta}^{\mathrm{op}} \to \FinBan\) to produce a first-quadrant bicomplex \((C_{p,q}(\mathcal{S}), \partial^{\mathrm{grad}}, \partial^{\mathrm{syn}})\).

2. Lawvere–Schreiber Cohesive Quadruple (Def. 5.14) Global sections \(\Gamma \colon \Xsyn \to \mathcal{S}\) extends to an adjoint quadruple where \(\Disc\) preserves finite products and \(\Pi(X \times Y) \simeq \Pi(X) \times \Pi(Y)\): \[ \Pi \;\dashv\; \Disc \;\dashv\; \Gamma \;\dashv\; \Codisc \qquad \left(\Xsyn \underset{\Disc,\, \Codisc}{\overset{\Pi,\, \Gamma}{\rightleftarrows}} \mathcal{S}\right) \]

3. Shape Modality \(\int \coloneqq \Disc \circ \Pi\) (Smooth Äondyne) For any Metroplex \(\mathcal{S} \in \Xsyn\), \(\int(\mathcal{S})\) extracts its smooth path-homotopy type (Äondyne).

4. Flat (\(\flat \coloneqq \Disc \circ \Gamma\)) & Sharp (\(\sharp \coloneqq \Codisc \circ \Gamma\)) Modalities \(\int \dashv \flat \dashv \sharp \colon \Xsyn \to \Xsyn\). Here \(\flat(\mathcal{S})\) extracts the underlying discrete combinatorial skeleton (Diskreter Enyphanmetroplex), while \(\sharp(\mathcal{S})\) embeds into the codiscrete envelope.

Manim Act IX: Lawvere–Schreiber Cohesive Modalities (Shape ∫, Flat ♭, Sharp ♯) on X_syn

Interactive Tectonic Spectral Sequence E^r_{p,q} & Metroplexdiabatik (Thm. 5.15)

Context (§5.3.5, Thm. 5.15): Filtering \(C_{p,q}(\mathcal{S})\) by vertical grade \(p\) yields \(E^2_{p,q} = H_p^{\mathrm{grad}}(H_q^{\mathrm{syn}}(C_{\bullet,\bullet})) \Rightarrow H_{p+q}(|\Tect_{\mathbf{Ban}}(\mathcal{S})|_{\mathbf{\Delta}})\) with differentials \(d^r \colon E^r_{p,q} \to E^r_{p-r, q+r-1}\).

Proof of Theorem 5.15 (Eilenberg–Zilber & Spectral Convergence): By the Eilenberg–Zilber–Cartier Theorem, \(N_\bullet(\operatorname{diag}^*\Tect_{\mathbf{Ban}}(\mathcal{S})) \simeq \operatorname{Tot}(C_{\bullet,\bullet}(\mathcal{S}))\) with \(D = \partial^{\mathrm{grad}} + (-1)^p \partial^{\mathrm{syn}}\). Since \(C_{p,q} = 0\) for \(p < 0\) or \(q < 0\), the column filtration \(F_p\operatorname{Tot}_m = \bigoplus_{i \le p} C_{i, m-i}\) is bounded below and exhaustive, guaranteeing finite-stage convergence \(E^\infty_{p,q} \cong \operatorname{Gr}_p H_{p+q}(\operatorname{Tot})\) (Mac Lane 1998). A non-vanishing differential \(d^r \ne 0\) (\(r \ge 2\)) is the exact homological obstruction to lifting a gradual \(p\)-cycle across \(r\) vertical grades without modifying syndrome level by \(r-1\) (Metroplexdiabatik, SM p. 103). \(\blacksquare\)

Carnap 5.1 (Scale-Relativity & Resolving Kim’s Downward Causation): Within Ontic Structural Realism (Ladyman & Ross 2007; French 2014), Theorem 5.15 and \((\phi_{n,n+N})_! \dashv (\phi_{n,n+N})^*\) dissolve Jaegwon Kim’s causal exclusion paradox: macro-scale constraint is not an anomalous force violating grade-\(0\) dynamics, but the transgression differential \(d^r\) and right-adjoint restriction \((\phi_{n,n+N})^*\) selecting globally compatible cohomology classes in \(\operatorname{Tot}(C_{\bullet,\bullet})\).

§5.3.6–5.3.7: Slice Totality \(T_n\), Protosimplexe & \(EN\)-Operator

STRATUM A/B: SLICE TOTALITIES (DEF. 5.16), PROTOSIMPLEXE (DEF. 5.17) & EN-OPERATOR (DEF. 5.18)

Definition 5.16: State Totality & Grade-\(n\) Slice Category \(T_n\) For a generative blueprint object \(G_n \in \operatorname{Obj}(\Met_n(\Xsyn))\), the structured realization category of grade-\(n\) state totality \(\mathsf{Tot}_n\) is the slice category: \[ T_n \;\coloneqq\; \Met_n(\Xsyn) / G_n \] * Terminal Object: \(\operatorname{id}_{G_n} \colon G_n \to G_n\). * Initial Object (Nullmetroplex \({}^n\mathbf{M}_0\)): \((0_n \to G_n)\) modeling structural exhaustion.

Definition 5.17: Augmented Subobject-Minimal Protosimplex Let \(\Alg_{\On}^0\) be the category of augmented algebras over the base constant subalgebra \(\Free_{\On}(\emptyset)\). An object \(\mathcal{P} \in \operatorname{Obj}(T_n)\) is a Protosimplex of Grade \(n\) if \(\mathcal{P} \not\cong \Free_{\On}(\emptyset)\) and it has no proper non-trivial augmented sub-algebras: \[ \forall A \hookrightarrow \mathcal{P} \text{ in } \Alg_{\On}^0, \qquad A \cong \Free_{\On}(\emptyset) \quad \lor \quad A \cong \mathcal{P} \]

Definition 5.18: Endogenous Synthesis (\(\SynEN\)) & Absorption (\(\AbsEN\)) \[ \SynEN_{p,q}^n \colon \Met_p(\Xsyn) \times \Met_q(\Xsyn) \longrightarrow \Met_n(\Xsyn) \qquad (p + q = n,\; q > 0) \] \[ \AbsEN_n^{p+q} \colon \Met_n(\Xsyn) \times \Met_{p+q}(\Xsyn) \longrightarrow \Met_n(\Xsyn) \qquad (p + q \le n,\; q > 0) \] Strict inequality \(p + q \le n\) prevents infinite recursive runaway during combination.

structure EndogenousAdmissible (n p q : ℕ) : Prop where
  grade_bound : p + q ≤ n
  pos_shift   : 0 < q

/-- Syntroclinic bridge hom-set adjunction equivalence (Def. 5.7, Prop. 5.8) -/
def syntroclinicHomEquiv {C D : Type*} [Category C] [Category D]
    {B : C ⥤ D} {R : D ⥤ C} (adj : B ⊣ R) (X : C) (Y : D) :
    (B.obj X ⟶ Y) ≃ (X ⟶ R.obj Y) :=
  adj.homEquiv X Y

§6.1.1: Metroplexäondyne, Mono-/Polydromie & Area (SM-27)

CULMINATION OF TEIL A & STRATUM D (SM, PP. 104–108, EQ. 27)

Dynamical Evolution in the Äondynentensorium

  • Chapter 5 constructed the multi-scale \((\infty, 1)\)-topos \(\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{syn}}\), yet higher homotopy remains directionless without an asymptotic selection principle.
  • When a grade-\(n\) Metroplex \(\overset{\scriptscriptstyle n}{\mathbf{M}}\widetilde{\mathrm{a}}(t_1, \dots, t_Q)\) evolves across an \(N\)-dimensional parameter space (Äondynentensorium), Heim terms it a Metroplexäondyne evolving in two distinct regimes:
    1. Monodromie (Monodromy): Unique, deterministic trajectory from any given initial state.
    2. Polydromie (Polydromy): At critical bifurcation loci (Polydromiepunkte \(x_0\)), trajectories branch into an exploratory tuft of \(P_\mu > 1\) potential paths.

The Recursive Äonische Area Formula (SM Eq. 27, p. 106): \[ AR_q \equiv AR_{(T_1)}^{(T_2)}\!\left[ (AR_{q-1})_{\gamma_{q-1}} \right]_1^{P_{q-1}} \quad \lor \quad AR_1 \equiv AR_{(T)}^{(T')}\!\left[ \overset{\scriptscriptstyle n}{\mathbf{M}}\widetilde{\mathrm{a}}(t_i)_1^Q \right] \tag{SM-27} \] An Area of order \(q\) (\(\mathfrak{AR}_q\)) is a recursive fiber bundle of \(P_{q-1}\) sub-areas of order \(q-1\) bounded and oriented by guiding attractor manifolds \((T_1, T_2)\).

§6.1.2–6.1.4: Telezentrik, Televarianz vs. Dysvarianz & \(C(m)\)

STRATUM D: TELEZENTREN, EXTINKTIONSDISKRIMINANTE (SM, PP. 108–116) & TRANSZENDENZSTUFEN C(m) (PP. 117–121)

Telezentrik, Telezentren (\(T_z\)) & The Kollektor (\(\mathfrak{Col}\), SM pp. 108–112): * Evolutionary curves in an äonische Area are channeled by distinguished asymptotic submanifolds of maximal coherence and minimal entropy—Telezentren (\(T_z\)). * Directly anticipates Karl Friston’s Free-Energy Principle (2010) and Andy Clark’s Active Inference (2016): self-organizing systems restrict trajectories to viable attracting sets by minimizing a Lyapunov bound on surprise along forward local gradients (\(-\grad\Psi\)). * Monodrome Area: Single globally dominant \(T_z\). Polydrome Area: Competing \(\{T_{z,1}, \dots, T_{z,K}\}\) separated by Morse–Smale boundaries. Reconverging tufts meet at a Kollektor (\(\mathfrak{Col}\)).

Televarianz, Dysvarianz (Prop. 6.12) & Extinktionsdiskriminante (\(\Dext\)): * Televarianten (\(\mathbf{W}_{\mathrm{tele}} \subset \mathcal{B}(T_z)\)): Preserve internal telezentrische Tektonik (Televarianzbedingung: \(\mathcal{B}(T_z) \ne \emptyset\); otherwise pseudotelezentrisch). * Dysvarianten (\(\mathbf{W}_{\mathrm{dys}}\)): Cross \(\Dext = \partial\mathcal{B}(T_z)\) into structural or functional decay: 1. Functional Dysvarianz: Kombinat rule architecture remains intact, while syndrome populations cease to be defined (initial or intermittent extinction). 2. Structural Dysvarianz: Internal Kombinat architecture fractures across \(\Dext\) (final extinction \(\to \nullsyntrix\)). Near \(\Dext\), metastable states trigger Resynkolation (SM, p. 114).

§6.1.4 Transzendenzstufen (\(C(m)\)) & Philosophical Preamble (SM, pp. 117–121): Stabilized Affinitätssyndrome (\(\mathfrak{A}\)) or Holoformen (\(\mathcal{H}\)) of ground area \(C(0)\) are lifted by extrasynkolative Transzendenzsynkolatoren (\(\Gamma_i\)) to higher qualitative strata \(C(m+1)\), governed by Transzendente Telezentralenrelativität: > “Die Telezentralen eines niedrigeren Transzendenzfeldes \(C(T-1)\) werden bei der Höhertranszendenz zu Nebentelezentralen des Feldes \(C(T)\).” (SM, pp. 117–118)

Goal-directed evolution is not a spooky retrocausal pull from the future, but the universal property of dynamic systems mapping local state observations into terminal behavioral invariants.

§6.2.1: Phase Manifolds \(\MAR\), Telecentre Dimensions & Collectors

STRATUM A: META-THEORETIC SCHOLIUM, DEFS. 6.1–6.5, PROP. 6.3 & COLLECTOR CYCLE (FIG. 6.1)

Scholium (Meta-Theoretic Justification: Terminal Coalgebras for Telezentrik): Why model Heim’s telecentric attractors through Universal Coalgebra rather than mechanical forces? 1. Non-Retrocausal Teleology: Mechanical forces push states forward blindly; naive teleology invokes unphysical backward signals. Coalgebras \((S, \alpha \colon S \to \mathcal{T}(S))\) classify states by their forward observable trajectory streams via the unique final morphism \(!_\alpha \colon S \to \nu\mathcal{T}\). 2. Riemannian Basin Geometry: Equipping \((\MAR, \gAR)\) with Lyapunov potential \(\Psi\) translates coalgebraic terminality into verifiable Polyak–Łojasiewicz gradient dissipation.

Defs. 6.1–6.5 & Prop. 6.3: Area Manifold, Basins, Dimensions & Collectors * Area Manifold (Def. 6.1) & Basin (Def. 6.2): Complete Riemannian manifold \((\MAR, \gAR)\) with smooth flow \(\Phi_t\) generated by \(\XAR\), and attractor basin \(\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{A}) \coloneqq \{x \mid \lim_{t\to\infty}\dist_{\gAR}(\Phi_t(x), \mathcal{A}) = 0\}\). * Dimensional Taxonomy (Prop. 6.3): \(\gamma = \dim\mathcal{A} \in \{0, 1, 2, 3\}\) classifies Punkt-, Linien-, Flächen-, and Raumtelezentren (without restricting global topology). Open panoramas adjoin ideal boundary points at \(\infty\) as projective telecentres (Def. 6.5). * Far-Centered Collector (Def. 6.4): Unstable tuft \(W^u_{\mathrm{tele}}(x_0) = W^u(x_0)\setminus\{x_0\} \cong \mathbb{R}^{P_\mu}\setminus\{0\}\) (\(P_\mu > 1\)) from Polydromy saddle \(x_0 \in \Dext\) is captured by Collector \(\mathcal{K}\) at separation \(L > 0\): \(W^u_{\mathrm{tele}}(x_0) \subseteq \mathcal{B}(\mathcal{K})\).

§6.2.2: Final Telecentric Coalgebra \((\nu\mathcal{T}, \zeta)\) & Lambek Proof

STRATUM A/B: PATH COALGEBRA (DEF. 6.6), FINAL COALGEBRA PROOF (THM. 6.7), LAMBEK LEMMA (PROP. 6.8) & CARNAP 6.1

Intuition Box (Telezentrik as a Mountain Basin) & Scholium (Algebra vs. Coalgebra): * Mountain Basin Analogy: Raindrops falling in a mountain watershed are not pulled by a mystical message from the lake at the valley floor; the local slope (\(-\grad\Psi\)) channels every drop inside the ridge (\(\Dext\)) toward the same terminal lake (\(T_z\)). * Algebraic Generation vs. Coalgebraic Observation: Algebras (\(T_{\mathsf{P}}(X) \to X\)) fold parts inward (bottom-up construction in Chapters 2–5). Coalgebras (\(S \to \mathcal{T}(S)\)) unfold state transitions outward in time (Rutten 2000; Jacobs 2016).

Interactive 4-Tab Inspector: Final Coalgebra \((\nu\mathcal{T}, \zeta)\) & Carnap 6.1

1. Basepoint-Preserving Telecentric Path Signature \(\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{R}}\) (Def. 6.6) In the locally presentable category of diffeological spaces \(\mathbf{Diff}_{\mathrm{smooth}}\) (Souriau 1980; Iglesias-Zemmour 2013), fix step \(\epsilon > 0\): \[ \mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{R}}(M) \;\coloneqq\; \Big( M \times C^\infty\big([0, \epsilon], M\big) \Big)_{\operatorname{ev}_0 = \operatorname{pr}_1} \;\amalg\; \mathbf{1} \] where \(\operatorname{ev}_0(\gamma) = \gamma(0) = x\) enforces basepoint continuity and \(\mathbf{1} = \{\ast_{\mathrm{term}}\}\) models \(\nullsyntrix\).

2. Proof of Theorem 6.7 (Existence of Final Coalgebra \((\nu\mathcal{T}, \zeta)\)) Since \(C^\infty([0,\epsilon], -)\) is a representable hom-functor in cartesian closed \(\mathbf{Diff}_{\mathrm{smooth}}\), it preserves connected limits; finite products and coproducts with \(\mathbf{1}\) commute with \(\omega^{\mathrm{op}}\)-limits. Thus the Adámek–Aczel terminal sequence \(\mathbf{1} \leftarrow \mathcal{T}\mathbf{1} \leftarrow \mathcal{T}^2\mathbf{1} \leftarrow \cdots\) stabilizes at \(\omega\): \(\nu\mathcal{T} \coloneqq \lim_{k<\omega}\mathcal{T}^k(\mathbf{1})\) carries a unique universal mediating arrow \(!_\alpha \colon S \to \nu\mathcal{T}\) for every \((S, \alpha)\). \(\blacksquare\)

3. Proof of Proposition 6.8 (Banach Fixed Point & Lambek Isomorphism) For any \((S, \alpha)\), the cone maps \(\alpha_k \colon S \to \mathcal{T}^k(\mathbf{1})\) (\(\alpha_{k+1} = \mathcal{T}(\alpha_k) \circ \alpha\)) induce the unique fixed point \(!_\alpha = \zeta^{-1} \circ \mathcal{T}(!_\alpha) \circ \alpha\). Regarding \((\mathcal{T}\nu\mathcal{T}, \mathcal{T}\zeta)\) as a coalgebra yields \(\eta \colon \mathcal{T}\nu\mathcal{T} \to \nu\mathcal{T}\) with \(\zeta\eta = \mathcal{T}(\eta\zeta)\). Since \(\zeta\) is a coalgebra map, \(\eta\zeta = \operatorname{id}_{\nu\mathcal{T}}\) by finality, so \(\zeta\eta = \mathcal{T}(\operatorname{id}) = \operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{T}\nu\mathcal{T}}\). \(\blacksquare\)

4. Carnap 6.1: Eliminating Retrocausal Metaphysics Two states \(x_1, x_2 \in S\) are telecentrically equivalent iff \(!_\alpha(x_1) = !_\alpha(x_2)\) in \(\nu\mathcal{T}\). Combined with Friston/Clark free-energy dissipation, Telezentrik is completely reduced to forward differential geometry and coalgebraic behavioral equivalence!

import Mathlib
open CategoryTheory
namespace Syntrometrie.StratumB.Telezentrik

structure Coalgebra (T : C ⥤ C) where
  carrier : C
  str     : carrier ⟶ T.obj carrier

/-- Final Telecentric Coalgebra (νT, ζ) certifying unique mediating arrow !ₐ (Thm. 6.7) -/
structure FinalCoalgebra (T : C ⥤ C) where
  terminal   : Coalgebra T
  toTerminal : (S : Coalgebra T) → (S.carrier ⟶ terminal.carrier)
  comm       : ∀ S, S.str ≫ T.map (toTerminal S) = toTerminal S ≫ terminal.str
  uniq       : ∀ (S : Coalgebra T) (f : S.carrier ⟶ terminal.carrier),
                 S.str ≫ T.map f = f ≫ terminal.str → f = toTerminal S
end Syntrometrie.StratumB.Telezentrik

Stage V of VI Laboratory: Telecentric Polarimeter Phase-Locking

Context (§6.2.2 Running Example): Coupled polarimeters with relative phase \(\theta(t) \in [-\pi/2, \pi/2]\), Lyapunov potential \(\Psi(\theta) = 1 - \cos\theta\), and feedback law \(\dot{\theta} = -\sin\theta\) (\(\dot{\Psi} = -\sin^2\theta \le -\Psi\)). Initial θ(0) (deg): +75° Time t: 0.0s
Manim Act VI: Stage V Coupled Polarimeter Phase-Locking Ψ(t) ≤ e⁻ᵗΨ(0) to Telezentrum θ = 0

Definition 6.9 & Theorem 6.10: Strict Televariant Stability Theorem Let \(\mathcal{K}_c = \{V(x) \le c\} \subset \mathcal{B}(\mathcal{A})\) be a compact sublevel set of excess potential \(V(x) \coloneqq \Psi(x) - \Psi(\mathcal{A})\) satisfying: 1. Morse–Bott Quadratic Coercivity: \(V(x) \ge \frac{\mu}{2}\dist_{\gAR}(x, \mathcal{A})^2\) (\(\mu > 0\)). 2. Polyak–Łojasiewicz (PL) Gradient Dissipation: \(\dot{V}(x) = \mathcal{L}_{\XAR}V(x) \le -\gamma V(x)\) (\(\gamma > 0\)). Then \(\mathcal{K}_c\) is positively invariant and every televariant trajectory \(x_0 \in \mathcal{K}_c\) converges exponentially to the Telezentrum \(\mathcal{A}\): \[ \dist_{\gAR}\big(\Phi_t(x_0), \mathcal{A}\big) \;\le\; \sqrt{\frac{2V(x_0)}{\mu}}\, e^{-\frac{\gamma}{2}t} \;\xrightarrow{t\to\infty}\; 0 \]

Proof of Theorem 6.10 (Grönwall & Metric Coercivity Bound): Along any integral curve \(x(t) = \Phi_t(x_0)\) with \(x_0 \in \mathcal{K}_c\), \(\frac{d}{dt}V(x(t)) = \mathcal{L}_{\XAR}V(x(t)) \le -\gamma V(x(t)) \le 0\), so \(V(x(t)) \le V(x_0) \le c\) and \(x(t)\) remains in \(\mathcal{K}_c\) for all \(t \ge 0\). Multiplying by \(e^{\gamma t}\) gives \(\frac{d}{dt}[e^{\gamma t}V(x(t))] \le 0\), which integrates by Grönwall’s Lemma to \(V(\Phi_t(x_0)) \le V(x_0)e^{-\gamma t}\). Combining with Morse–Bott quadratic coercivity \(\frac{\mu}{2}\dist_{\gAR}(\Phi_t(x_0), \mathcal{A})^2 \le V(\Phi_t(x_0))\) and taking square roots yields the exact exponential decay rate \(\gamma/2\). \(\blacksquare\)

Remark (Toy Example: 1D Double-Well Telecentric Potential): On \(\MAR = \mathbb{R}\) with \(\Psi(x) = (x^2 - 1)^2\) and \(\dot{x} = -\Psi'(x) = -4x(x^2 - 1)\), the critical set \(\Crit(\Psi) = \{-1, 0, +1\}\) splits into two stable Punkttelezentren \(T_{z,1} = -1\), \(T_{z,2} = +1\) (\(\Psi''(\pm 1) = 8 > 0\)) with televariant basins \((-\infty, 0)\) and \((0, +\infty)\), separated by the unstable saddle \(p_0 = 0\) (\(\Psi''(0) = -4 < 0\)) forming the Extinktionsdiskriminante \(\Dext = \{0\}\).

Act VII (Fig. 6.3): 3D Lyapunov Landscape
Act VIII: Morse–Smale Separatrix D_ext & Foliations

Theorem 6.13 (Telecentre Dimension Bounds & Foliation, SM pp. 117–118): In an \(n\)-dimensional Tensorium, every Telezentrum satisfies \(0 \le \gamma_k \le n - 1\): * Equal Dimensions (\(\gamma_1 = \gamma_2\)): Connected by a unique Telezentrale of dimension \(\gamma_1 + 1\). * Unequal Dimensions (\(\gamma_2 > \gamma_1\)): Connected by a \((\gamma_2 - \gamma_1)\)-parameter family of \((\gamma_1 + 1)\)-dimensional Telezentralen foliating a region of dimension \(\gamma_2 + 1\).

Theorem 6.14 & Proof (Morse–Smale Stratified Separatrix Boundary Theorem): For a Morse–Smale gradient system \(\XAR = -\grad_{\gAR}\Psi\) on compact \((\MAR, \gAR)\), the Extinktionsdiskriminante \(\Dext \coloneqq \partial\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{A})\) is the Whitney-stratified union of stable manifolds of hyperbolic saddles \(p_r \in \Crit(\Psi)\) of Morse index \(\ind(p_r) \ge 1\): \[ \Dext \;=\; \overline{\bigcup_{\ind(p_j)=1} W^s(p_j)} \;=\; \bigcup_{r \,:\, \ind(p_r)\ge 1} W^s(p_r), \qquad \dim W^s(p_j) = n - 1 \] Proof: By the Stable Manifold Theorem for Morse–Smale gradient flows (Palis & de Melo 1982), \(\MAR = \bigsqcup_{p \in \Crit(\Psi)} W^s(p)\) where \(\operatorname{codim} W^s(p) = \ind(p)\). Open basins \(\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{A}_k)\) are unions of index-\(0\) stable manifolds; their topological boundary \(\partial\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{A})\) is invariant and must consist of stable manifolds of codimension \(\ge 1\) (\(\ind(p_r) \ge 1\)). By the \(\lambda\)-Lemma (Inclination Lemma), transversality \(W^u(p_r) \pitchfork W^s(p_j)\) guarantees that the frontier \(\overline{W^s(p_j)} \setminus W^s(p_j)\) is a Whitney-stratified union of higher-index stable manifolds \(W^s(p_r)\) (\(\ind(p_r) \ge 2\)). \(\blacksquare\)

Theorem 6.16 (Branching Types of Extinction Boundary, SM pp. 113–114): Initial and final dysvariance correspond to a single-branch extinction boundary \(\Dext\), whereas intermittent dysvariance corresponds to a two-branch boundary enclosing a metastable recovery zone where Resynkolation restores televariance!

§6.3.3: Transzendente Telezentralenrelativität & Full Proof

STRATUM B: TRANSCENDENCE HIERARCHY (DEF. 6.15), THMS. 6.17–6.18 & RELATIVITY PROOF (THM. 6.19, FIG. 6.4)

Definition 6.15 & Theorem 6.19: Transzendente Telezentralenrelativität Let \(\mathbf{Trans}\) be the hierarchy of transcendence strata \(C(m)\) modeled by coalgebra categories \(\mathcal{C}_m \coloneqq \Coalg(\mathcal{T}_m)\) with terminal Telezentrum \((\nu\mathcal{T}_m, \zeta_m)\). The Transzendenzsynkolator \(\Gamma_m \colon \mathcal{C}_m \to \mathcal{C}_{m+1}\) is left adjoint to the forgetful functor \(U_{m+1}\): \[ \Gamma_m \;\dashv\; U_{m+1} \colon \Coalg(\mathcal{T}_{m+1}) \longrightarrow \Coalg(\mathcal{T}_m) \] Under this adjunction, the terminal Haupttelezentrum of stratum \(C(m)\) is mapped to a non-terminal basal Nebentelezentrum in \(C(m+1)\): \[ \boxed{\Gamma_m(\nu\mathcal{T}_m) \;\cong\; \HypMet_{m+1} \;\not\cong\; \nu\mathcal{T}_{m+1} \quad\text{in }\mathcal{C}_{m+1}} \]

Proof of Theorem 6.19 (Left-Adjoint Non-Preservation of Terminal Coalgebras): 1. Basal Lifting: In stratum \(\mathcal{C}_m\), every televariant system \((S_m, \alpha_m)\) admits a unique coalgebra homomorphism \(!_{\alpha_m} \colon S_m \to \nu\mathcal{T}_m\). 2. Transcendence Encapsulation: Applying the Transzendenzsynkolator \(\Gamma_m \colon \mathcal{C}_m \to \mathcal{C}_{m+1}\) encapsulates the stabilized terminal invariants \(\nu\mathcal{T}_m\) as the initial free generator \(\HypMet_{m+1} \coloneqq \Gamma_m(\nu\mathcal{T}_m)\) of the higher-order coalgebra category \(\mathcal{C}_{m+1}\). 3. Categorical Relativity: By general category theory (Mac Lane 1998), left adjoints preserve colimits and initial objects, but do not preserve terminal objects or final coalgebras. Since \(\Gamma_m(\nu\mathcal{T}_m)\) is an initial generator in \(\mathcal{C}_{m+1}\) while \(\nu\mathcal{T}_{m+1}\) is terminal, \(\Gamma_m(\nu\mathcal{T}_m) \not\cong \nu\mathcal{T}_{m+1}\) whenever \(\mathcal{C}_{m+1}\) is non-degenerate. \(\blacksquare\)

Theorems 6.17 & 6.18 (Pure Monodromy & Even Secondary Parity): * Thm. 6.17: Higher transcendence strata \(C(m \ge 1)\) are purely monodromic apart from secondary and main telecentres; purely extrasynkolative fields occur at stage \(1\), while higher stages are mixed (SM, pp. 110–111). * Thm. 6.18: The number of secondary telecentres \(\mu \in 2\mathbb{N}\) is strictly even (SM, p. 119).

Interactive Telezentralenrelativität Stratum Escalator (Fig. 6.4)

Context (§6.3.3, Thm. 6.19): Step across transcendence strata \(C(0) \to C(1) \to C(2) \to C(3)\) to see how what functions as an absolute terminal Haupttelezentrum (\(\nu\mathcal{T}_m\)) in \(C(m)\) is demoted by left adjoint \(\Gamma_m\) into a basal Nebentelezentrum (\(\HypMet_{m+1}\)) in \(C(m+1)\).

§6.3.4: Morse–Poincaré–Hopf Parity (\(\mu \in 2\mathbb{N}\)) & Wasserstein Resynkolation

STRATUM B: MORSE–POINCARÉ–HOPF PARITY PROOF (THM. 6.20) & WASSERSTEIN KRAMERS RESYNKOLATION PROOF (THM. 6.21)

Interactive Morse–Poincaré–Hopf Even Parity Explorer (Thm. 6.20)

Context (Thm. 6.20, SM pp. 119–120): On a compact isolating panorama \(\Omega \subset \MAR\) with \(\chi(\Omega) = 0\) and inward gradient flow, \(\sum_{k=0}^n (-1)^k c_k = \chi(\Omega) = 0 \implies c_0 = c_1\), so total secondary centers \(\mu = c_0 + c_1 = 2c_0\) is strictly even, paired by \(\mu/2\) geodetic Telezentralen!

Attractors c0: 3 
    Lean 4: <code>telecentre_even_parity : (c0 = c1) → c0 + c1 = 2 * c0</code>

Proof of Theorem 6.20 (Morse Homology & Mod-2 Parity): By the Morse Homology Theorem on compact manifolds with inflowing boundary (\(\langle \grad\Psi, \nu_{\mathrm{out}}\rangle > 0\) on \(\partial\Omega\)), the Morse–Smale–Witten complex \((CM_\bullet(\Omega, \Psi), \partial_{\mathrm{MS}})\) has homology \(H_\bullet(\Omega; \mathbb{Z})\). Taking alternating ranks gives \(\sum_{k=0}^n (-1)^k c_k = \chi(\Omega) = 0\). Since \((-1)^k \equiv 1 \pmod 2\), reducing mod \(2\) gives \(\mu_{\mathrm{total}} = \sum_k c_k \equiv 0 \pmod 2\). When \(c_{k \ge 2} = 0\), \(c_0 = c_1 \implies \mu = 2c_0\), and each 1D unstable manifold \(\overline{W^u(p_j)}\) connects two attractors \(T_{z,a}, T_{z,b}\) as a geodetic Telezentrale. \(\blacksquare\)

Context (Thm. 6.21, SM p. 114): Adjust lowest index-\(1\) saddle barrier height \(\Delta E_{\mathrm{barrier}} = \Psi(p^*) - \Psi(\mathcal{A})\) and noise variance \(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{fluct}}\). When \(\Delta E_{\mathrm{barrier}} / \varepsilon_{\mathrm{fluct}} < 2.0\), click Trigger Resynkolation (\(\Rresyn\)) to switch Komplexsynkolator intervals!

Barrier ΔEbarrier: 0.60 Noise εfluct: 0.45

Theorem 6.21 & Proof (Wasserstein Free-Energy Flow & Eyring–Kramers Resynkolation): Under Itô Langevin dynamics \(dx_t = -\grad\Psi\,dt + \sqrt{2\varepsilon_{\mathrm{fluct}}}\,dW_t\), the Fokker–Planck equation \(\partial_t\rho_t = \operatorname{div}_g(\rho_t\grad(\Psi + \varepsilon_{\mathrm{fluct}}\ln\rho_t))\) is the \(W_2\)-steepest descent of \(\Ffree[\rho] = \int \Psi\rho\,d\operatorname{vol}_g + \varepsilon_{\mathrm{fluct}}\int \rho\ln\rho\,d\operatorname{vol}_g\), integrating by parts to the exact Fisher-information dissipation identity: \[ \frac{d}{dt}\Ffree[\rho_t] \;=\; -\int_{\MAR} \big\|\grad_{\gAR}(\Psi + \varepsilon_{\mathrm{fluct}}\ln\rho_t)\big\|_{\gAR}^2 \rho_t\,d\operatorname{vol}_g \;\le\; 0 \] Laplace saddle-point asymptotics on the Dirichlet form across lowest index-\(1\) saddle \(p^* \in \Dext\) yields the Eyring–Kramers survival time \(\tau_{\mathrm{surv}} \simeq \frac{2\pi}{|\lambda_1(p^*)|}\sqrt{\frac{|\det\Hess\Psi(p^*)|}{\det\Hess\Psi(\mathcal{A})}}\exp(\Delta E_{\mathrm{barrier}}/\varepsilon_{\mathrm{fluct}})\), triggering \(\Rresyn \colon (\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{syn}}, \Psi) \mapsto (\mathcal{O}'_{\mathrm{syn}}, \Psi')\) when \(\Delta E_{\mathrm{barrier}} < \varepsilon_{\mathrm{fluct}}\ln(\tau_{\min}/\tau_0)\). \(\blacksquare\)

Physical & Epistemic Sanity Checks (§6.5): 1. Unpolarized Flat Potential Baseline Check (\(\Psi = \text{const}\)): * \(\grad\Psi = 0 \implies \XAR = 0\), and the flow is static identity \(\Phi_t = \operatorname{id}\). * No finite telecenters exist (\(\mathcal{A} = \emptyset\)), and the system becomes an unpolarized projective panorama (pseudotelezentrisch) with improper telecenters at infinity, matching Heim’s limit (SM, p. 108). 2. Adjunction Non-Preservation Sanity Check: * Left adjoints \(\Gamma_m \dashv U_{m+1}\) preserve initial objects and colimits, but systematically fail to preserve terminal objects (\(\Gamma_m(\mathbf{1}) \not\cong \mathbf{1}\)), guaranteeing that teleological goals cannot remain absolute across transcendence levels.

Carnapian Explication 6.1: Telezentrik as Terminal Coalgebraic Invariant 1. Explicandum (\(\mathbf{D}\)): Historical notion of attraction toward future-oriented target states (SM, p. 108). 2. Explicatum (\(\mathbf{A}/\mathbf{B}\)): Final coalgebra \((\nu\mathcal{T}, \zeta)\) and unique universal map \(!_\alpha \colon S \to \nu\mathcal{T}\). 3. Exactness & Simplicity: Lambek’s lemma (\(\zeta \colon \nu\mathcal{T} \xrightarrow{\sim} \mathcal{T}(\nu\mathcal{T})\)) together with Polyak–Łojasiewicz dissipation \(\dot{V} \le -\gamma V\). 4. Fruitfulness: Retrocausality is eliminated; attractor basins, Morse–Smale separatrices \(\Dext\), and Eyring–Kramers Resynkolation become verifiable dynamical invariants.

Chapter 6 Deliverables: Telecentric Coalgebras & Completion of Teil A * Coalgebraic Teleology: Telezentren are terminal coalgebras \((\nu\mathcal{T}, \zeta)\) inducing unique universal homomorphisms \(!_\alpha \colon S \to \nu\mathcal{T}\) from local dynamical systems without retrocausality. * Televariance & Extinction: Exponential Polyak–Łojasiewicz Lyapunov dissipation (\(\dot{V} \le -\gamma V\)), Whitney-stratified Morse–Smale separatrices (\(\Dext = \partial\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{A})\)), and Wasserstein Eyring–Kramers Resynkolation classify stable, metastable, and extinct trajectories. * Relativity of Purpose: Adjacent transcendence strata exchange terminal attractors and initial building blocks through left-adjoint liftings (\(\Gamma_m(\nu\mathcal{T}_m) \cong \HypMet_{m+1} \not\cong \nu\mathcal{T}_{m+1}\)).

The Epistemological Obstruction: Empirical Intractability of Pure Logic With Chapter 6, the universal categorical logic of Teil A is complete. Yet pure categorical logic produces structural invariants without numerical coordinates, physical units of length or mass, differential field tensors, or laboratory predictions.

The Functorial Ascent to Chapter 7 (Teil B): The Anthropomorphic Pivot to Number Fields To interface this abstract machinery with empirical science, Chapter 7 (Anthropomorphic Syntrometry) executes the Anthropomorphic Pivot: restricting the observer site to the algebraic number fields \(\mathbf{Coeff} = [\mathbb{Q} \subseteq \mathbb{R} \subseteq \mathbb{C}]\), applying the realification functor \((V)_{\mathbb{R}}\), and clothing pure numbers with physical dimensions via the Semantic Iterator \(S_n ; \widetilde{a} = R_n\) (SM Eq. 28).
